⏺ Done. docs/research_program.md, 77,000 words, eleven sections.

  A correction I owe you. I pitched P6's neural-collapse hook — that κ̂ is a
  better-specified NC1 metric — as its strongest idea. The re-review found it's
  largely prior art: per-class vMF fitting of classifier features with an
  estimated κ is already SIREN (NeurIPS 2022), KappaFace, CIDER, and the
  vMF-mixture-softmax line, and directional NC1 is HUG/GNC. What's actually
  unclaimed is narrower — the null-calibrated admissibility boundary and the
  p-sweep. That's a methods contribution, not CVPR. P6's venue is now marked
  "see §11" rather than CVPR, and it needs either narrowing or a genuinely new
  claim before spending its 99 hours. Its detection half separately faces
  Carlini & Wagner (2017) and Tramèr (ICML 2022) with OOD-era baselines, no
  corruption control, and no held-out attack.

  On seeds — you were right and the fix is better than what I'd have gotten by 
  just capping the number. P3 now runs 5 seeds at 4B, 3 at 9B, and recovers
  resolution from CRN pairing, rank pooling, and per-problem mixed effects over
  MBPP-Plus's 378 problems. Critically, it also found that per-problem pooling
  doesn't help as much as the VTAB-19 analogy suggested, and drew the honest
  conclusion: the interaction is demoted to secondary, and the pre-registered
  primary claim is now the simple effect at MDE 1.4. As its own text puts it — a
  design that needs 12 seeds to say anything is a design whose analysis is
  wrong, not whose budget is small.

  The re-review's other three findings, all live:

  - P3's 1.4 MDE rests on an assumed CRN ρ=0.7, which is a simulation number,
  not measured — and ρ̂ can't be estimated from the budgeted calibration. At
  ρ=0.4 it's 1.9 against a modal effect of +1–2.5. The underpowered problem
  moved from the interaction into the simple effect rather than vanishing.
  - P6's Gate G0 contradicts itself: it gates the robustness half on a 1.6–2.5
  point robust-accuracy difference between two non-adversarially-trained
  ResNet-18s — a quantity that's either 0.0% vs 0.0% or exactly the masking
  artifact another clause of the same gate exists to catch.
  - Compute is inconsistent by ~5%: P2 and P3 price the same 4B→9B jump at 2.4×
  and 1.14×. Real total ~1,900–1,980 across two card types. Changes no ordering.

  Program stands at ~1,900 GPU-hours with all six gates openable for ~42.

⏺ Agent "Fable review of research program" finished · 6m 47s

⏺ Review is back. It deliberately skipped re-litigating what §11 already caught
  and went after what the document doesn't say about itself. Four findings I
  think are right and hadn't been surfaced:

  1. The portfolio's own priors contradict its venue table. P1 puts ~50% on
  "it's an effective-LR artifact" and ~40% on "it's the cosine classifier." P2
  gives 25% to the only branch producing the ICLR paper. P3's own expected-spend
  math implies Gate 1 passes with ~16.5%. P6 calls the masking artifact "the
  outcome we consider most likely." Multiply those out and the expected program
  is roughly one methods paper (P4), one or two honest negatives, and workshop 
  notes — not CVPR + 4×ICLR + CVPR. Each gate is individually well-calibrated;
  nobody multiplied them. Staffing and calendar are being planned against the
  optimistic branch.

  2. The downside protection is triple-counted. P1 §1.8 Tier M, P4 §4.2, and P6
  §6.2 each claim the null-calibrated κ feasibility boundary as their fallback
  headline. §11 noticed the collision; it understated the consequence. When P1
  and P6's positive claims both die — jointly likely on the plan's own priors —
  both pivot onto ground P4 already occupies, and two of the three pivots are
  worth nothing. "A negative result is publishable here" appears ~15 times in
  the document. It's true at most once.

  3. P2 and P3 are built on refuting an unpublished, anonymous draft. nLoRA is
  an ICLR 2026 submission that doesn't cite DeLoRA — decisions are long out, so
  it was plausibly rejected. P2 §2.4 calls the tuned-baseline deflation of the
  +7.63 "the single most-cited thing this project could produce." Cited by whom?
  The field never absorbed the claim; you can't deflate one nobody holds. The
  real anchor is DeLoRA, against which the delta at r=2 is one scalar per rank 
  per layer. That's checkable in an hour and it changes P2's motivation.

  4. The mechanism claims bottom out in underpowered correlations at the wrong 
  scale. The ELR controls are genuinely better than published practice — but
  every mechanism test is a rank correlation on a handful of points (P1 Gate
  1(ii) reduces to 9 paired points, p≈0.17 at threshold). The only controls that
  intervene rather than correlate — angular-update matching, hard
  renormalization — run at MLP scale, while the mechanism claim gets made at
  ImageNet and 9B.

  On the four axes you asked about:

  Datasets — two live problems. commonsense170k's eight tasks are pre-2020 and
  thoroughly contaminated; a tuned Qwen3.5-9B LoRA baseline starts in the high
  80s, and the draft's +7.63 lived on a 75.42 baseline. Report everything as
  error-rate reduction, not raw points. Worse for P3: the LiveCodeBench window 
  (2025-06 → 2026-05) is never checked against Qwen3.5's data cutoff, and
  MBPP/HumanEval are the two most contaminated code benchmarks in existence —
  the plan's own reported 70–88% base rates are partly memorization. Also
  Cora/Citeseer are deprecated in graph learning; P5 needs one OGB dataset.

  Metrics — mostly good, one clean catch: P1's plasticity metric is
  self-normalized ("steps to 90% of its own converged accuracy"), which flatters
  any arm that converges to a lower plateau. And P3's pass@16 co-primary is
  saturated at an 80% base rate — dead on arrival on MBPP-Plus, alive only on
  the hard slice.

  Benchmarks — P3's primary should be windowed LiveCodeBench; MBPP-Plus reads as
  legacy in 2026. P1's CVPR table has a social problem the caveats don't solve:
  reviewers pattern-match against iCaRL/DER/FOSTER numbers, and a
  67%-joint-accuracy table with none of those methods is a hard sell regardless
  of how honestly it's framed.

  Baselines — the sharpest section, three specific holes:
  - P2 doesn't run its own killer ablation. P3 §3.3 has the LoRA+diag(s) arm —
  per-component gain, no normalization — and calls it exactly that. P2, whose
  entire claim is separating normalization from per-component strength, has it
  nowhere in Tiers 0–2. Sibling projects disagreeing about the single
  load-bearing control.
  - SDC/LDC are cited in P1's motivation and never run. They're the direct
  competitor to the drift story — compensate drift post hoc vs. prevent it
  architecturally. If SDC on a plain backbone matches nMLP, the architecture
  contribution is dead.
  - FeTrIL/FeCAM run only at MLP scale. They're free on cached features, and
  they appear only where they can't threaten the claim. Plus AdaLoRA
  discussed-not-run (published per-component importance scaling, 2023, in PEFT)
  and no DoRA in the language tiers.

  No strawmen found — it noted the plan repeatedly does the opposite. The two de
  facto ones are P6's AT arms compared without per-regime LR tuning and P2's
  language tables without DoRA/AdaLoRA.

  Verdicts: P4 is the strongest project and should run first — coherent, cheap,
  self-contained, the only properly powered application. P1
  conditional-CVPR/expected-methods-paper, P2 expected TMLR, P3 expected
  workshop note, P5 expected TMLR, P6 not CVPR in any branch. Program-level:
  ~1,900 GPU-h serialized across one A6000 and one 3090 at ≤0.8 FTE is a 9–14
  month calendar supporting two to three papers, one of them strong.

  Three things worth doing before any real spend, in order: check nLoRA's actual
  publication status (an hour, and it rewrites P2/P3's motivation), verify the
  LCB window is post-cutoff, and pick the single owner of the measurement
  contribution. I can apply the review to the document, but the nLoRA status and
  the cutoff are facts to establish first — the edits depend on the answers.

✻ Crunched for 3m 43s

⏺ Agent "P1 continual learning extensions" finished · 4m 25s

⏺ P1 is back with the key upgrade: an angular-motion dial that intervenes on the
  mediator instead of correlating with it — clamp per-step rotation in the
  unnormalized net (no sphere anywhere), and dial the normalized net's eigen-LRs
  up, then check whether both architectures fall on one dose–response curve.
  That converts the drift story from a 9-point Spearman into an assigned-dose
  experiment, and it absorbs the LARS objection as a data point on the curve
  rather than a rebuttal. It also found that SDC is free on features the plan
  already caches, so the prevention-vs-compensation head-to-head costs ~8h.

  Waiting on the other five before the adversarial round.

✻ Brewed for 8s

⏺ Agent "P5 hyperspherical VAE extensions" finished · 4m 10s

⏺ P5 came back with the most consequential single finding so far: the plan's H2 
  is mathematically wrong. It states "KL grows roughly linearly in m at fixed
  κ." At fixed κ with κ≪m, KL ≈ κ²/2m — it vanishes. The correct statement is
  that informativeness requires κ = Θ(m), and there KL = Θ(m). That reframes the
  dimension wall as a rate-allocation bottleneck of any single-scalar 
  posterior, not a property of the sphere — which implies a ~3 GPU-h control arm
  nobody has run: a tied-σ isotropic Gaussian VAE. If that walls identically at
  d≥40, the 2018–2020 literature was explaining the wrong variable.

  It also killed a claim I'd have let through: hyperspherical VAEs are not
  structurally immune to posterior collapse — KL is exactly 0 at κ=0 and the
  gradient vanishes there, so κ→0 is reachable. The defensible version is that
  collapse is all-or-nothing in one scalar, hence detectable as a κ bifurcation
  and fixable with a single floor. Plus two prior-art traps the section cites
  neither of (Xu & Durrett 2018, Guu et al. 2018 — both used fixed-κ vMF as a
  collapse fix), which would be a one-line rejection.

  I'll have the adversarial round verify that asymptotic independently before
  any of it goes in the document. Four agents still out.

✻ Sautéed for 12s

⏺ Agent "P4 geometry measurement extensions" finished · 4m 44s

⏺ P4 delivered the resolution to the fallback collision, and it's better than a
  treaty: P4 ships vmf-measure v0.1 (null-quantile interpolant + admissibility
  calculator) as a versioned artifact by end of week 2 — the null-table campaign
  is CPU-only and off the GPU critical path. P1 needs its null in week 1;
  without that deliverable P1 builds a throwaway and the collision silently
  reopens. P1 then keeps a genuinely different estimand (angular displacement
  between training phases against a training-noise null, which P4 doesn't
  build), P6 keeps the p-sweep as empirical validation of P4's theory, and P2/P3
  are consumers.

  Two upgrades that raise its ceiling past "better ruler":

  - The audit becomes a census. Not 3 papers — a pre-registered harvest of
  ~60–120 quantitative claims from the isotropy and neural-collapse literatures,
  classified admissible/marginal/infeasible by table lookup at zero GPU cost,
  with a 12–20 claim reproduction stratum. Every per-class NC1 number on
  CIFAR-100 has n=500 < p=512.
  - Every instrument, not just κ. At n/p ~ O(1) the empirical spectrum is
  Marchenko–Pastur-deformed even for perfectly isotropic data, so effective rank
  and participation ratio are biased downward — toward "low-dimensional,
  anisotropic," which is again the direction of the claim being made. A
  six-instrument directional-bias table is a measurement theory of
  representation geometry rather than a vMF paper.

  It also flagged the known-mean estimator's null floor is √(p/n) vs p/√n — a √p
  extension of the resolvable region, 64× at p=4096. That one's already proven
  in the plan and buried.

  Three agents still out (P2, P3, P6).

✻ Cooked for 14s

⏺ Agent "P3 RL codegen extensions" finished · 5m 31s

⏺ P3 came back and endorsed your merge — with a sharper reason than I'd have
  given. The two projects are one bet placed twice: both papers' only novel
  delta is per-component vs global scale, on the same method. If it dies in P2's
  8-hour vision gate, P3's gate dies with it. And since DoLoRA is your own
  unpublished thesis, P2's fallback identity ("the most-citable tuned-baseline
  reproduction") has no external claim to reproduce — so P2 standalone collapses
  into "our own method, tested properly," which is what the merged paper
  already is.

  The merge also fixes the estimand problem you'd otherwise be stuck with.
  Instead of a 2×2 SFT-vs-RL interaction — which invites the fatal objection
  that the two regimes differ in a dozen ways besides signal density — it
  pre-registers a five-level dose axis on one substrate: SFT on gold →
  rejection-sampling FT → partial-credit RLVR → binary RLVR at G=8 → binary at
  G=4. Same prompts, same verifier, same token budget, CRN-coupled seeds. The
  primary becomes a slope, the seed random intercept drops out of it, and the
  implied endpoint MDE is ~1.3 pts at ρ=0.7 and ~1.8 at ρ=0.4 — better than the
  current interaction's 1.6/2.2, and it's the claim on the marquee. It also
  pre-registers measuring the dose axis by gradient SNR rather than assuming the
  ordering.

  One real bug it caught: the plan trains on LiveCodeBench-v6 and evaluates on 
  "LCB-v6 easy." That's a train–test leak inside the design, independent of any
  model-cutoff question.

  Two agents left (P2, P6), then the adversarial round.

✻ Churned for 12s

⏺ Agent "P6 robustness extensions" finished · 5m 10s

⏺ P6 back, and it found the audit's sharpest target: KappaFace fits per-class κ 
  at roughly n≈70 images/class at p=512 — n/p ≈ 0.13, which is ~75× below the
  admissibility boundary, in a regime where κ̂_null ≈ p/√n ≈ 60 and the bias
  points the same direction as the published claim. That's a concrete,
  checkable, inference-only audit on released ArcFace/CosFace models, ~3 GPU-h.

  It also settled the smoothing question I'd flagged as speculative. The
  certificate is derivable — the vMF likelihood ratio is exp(κ(μ₁−μ₂)ᵀz),
  monotone in a linear functional, so Neyman–Pearson worst-case sets are
  half-spaces intersected with the sphere and Cohen's two-point argument carries
  to a geodesic radius. It's non-vacuous in input space (≈0.5–0.9 L2 at
  tolerable noise doses). But it's honest about the content: via the
  tangent-space correspondence this is ≈ Cohen-Gaussian restricted to the
  sphere, and Yang et al. (ICML 2020) already cover broad noise families — so
  it's a section, not a headline. It also corrected the plan's difficulty
  estimate: the needed quantity is a 2-D integral, not the 1-D marginal the plan
  assumed.

  On your latent-noise seed, it found the use I think is actually right: not a
  defense but an instrument. Calibrated vMF noise is a dose with a closed-form
  readout (E[μᵀz] = A_p(κ)), which gives per-layer angular amplification
  profiles and lets you express adversarial perturbations in noise-equivalent 
  angular doses — then ask whether adversarial examples displace latents more
  than their accuracy cost predicts, or sit on the same curve as CIFAR-10-C
  corruption. Either answer is a result.

  One collision to resolve: P4 wants to absorb the NC1 audit, P6 wants to keep
  it. The adversarial round gets that.

  P2 is the last one out.

✻ Brewed for 16s

⏺ Agent "P2 DoLoRA extensions" finished · 6m 43s

⏺ All six back. P2's is the one you'll want to see first, because it checked the
  thesis PDF against what the plan assumed and found three things that change
  the project:

  The +7.63 is 74% one task. BoolQ alone goes 57.58 → 85.67, contributing +5.62
  of it. And a LoRA-r=2 BoolQ of 57.58 is ~25 points below LLaMA-3-8B's
  zero-shot — that baseline run was broken, not merely untuned. The LR grids 
  were disjoint: LoRA/DoRA swept {5e-4…1e-5}, DoLoRA {5e-2…1e-3}, and DoLoRA's
  selected 1e-2 is 100–200× LoRA's — so the headline comparison is confounded
  exactly at the mechanism, since decoupling is an effective-LR intervention.
  Also, the thesis trained on a 5-task concatenation scored by multiple-choice
  log-likelihood, not the 8-task generative protocol the plan was going to use,
  so the plan was set to replicate the wrong thing.

  That reframes the replication as genuinely valuable rather than ceremonial —
  and as it notes, retiring your own +7.63 against tuned baselines is a paper
  only you can write.

  Now the adversarial round. Three Opus agents, different model from the
  proposers:

⏺ 3 background agents launched (↓ to manage)
   ├ Adversarial review P1 and P5
   ├ Adversarial review merged P2 and P3
   └ Adversarial review P4 and P6

⏺ Three adversarial agents running on Opus, each tasked to attack rather than
  agree, and each given specific things to verify rather than accept:

  - P1 + P5 — must independently derive the KL(vMF‖Uniform) asymptotics with
  mpmath before that claim goes anywhere near an abstract, and attack whether
  "rotation-clamped SGD" is even well-defined (my worry: clamping weight
  rotation may silently change the effective LR, reintroducing the exact
  confound the intervention exists to remove — and weight rotation isn't the
  same object as representation drift, which is the actual mediator).
  - P2 + P3 — must check all five thesis findings against the PDF itself,
  including whether the BoolQ arithmetic holds. And attack the dose axis on the
  obvious objection: D1/D2 are token-level cross-entropy while D3/D4/D5 are
  policy-gradient with a group baseline, so they differ in gradient estimator,
  on- vs off-policy, KL regularization, and exposure to self-generated text —
  not just "bits per example." Plus whether defining the dose by gradient SNR
  makes the primary claim circular, given the mechanism story is about gradient
  SNR.
  - P4 + P6 — must adjudicate the audit-ownership collision and verify three
  pieces of arithmetic: the √(p/n) vs p/√n null floor, the Marchenko–Pastur
  direction claim, and the smoothing radius chain. It also gets the question I
  think is the real risk to the whole audit framing: KappaFace uses per-class κ 
  as a training-time weighting hyperparameter, not as an estimand. Attacking a
  weighting scheme with measurement theory may be a category error, and if it
  is, the sharpest audit target evaporates.

❯ For P2+P3, we found in the experiments that the model was much more robust to 
  high LR than LoRA/DoRA. Hence why we set it much higher. I am not sure the    
  training data makes sense to stick to, and I would suggest we reconsider from

⏺ That reframes the LR finding substantially, and in your favor.
  ⎿  Message queued for delivery to aebbad1134c63490c at its next tool 
     round.

⏺ Your LR point substantially defuses the confound criticism. Sweeping each arm
  over the range where it's actually stable is the correct protocol — the
  alternative (forcing all arms onto one grid) would have handicapped DoLoRA at
  LRs where it was fine and LoRA diverged. LoRA's grid topping out at 5e-4
  brackets the standard LoRA optimum, so it plausibly contained its own argmax.
  What survives is narrower: whether LoRA's grid actually contained its peak
  (checkable from the thesis's per-LR numbers), and the BoolQ 57.58 collapse,
  which indicates a broken run no tuning rationale excuses.

  But I think the finding is being under-sold as a protocol note. "Normalization
  widens the usable learning-rate band" is a better headline than "+X points on
  benchmark Y." It's mechanism-level, it's exactly what decoupling direction
  from magnitude predicts, it has direct practical payoff, and it's nearly free
  — it's the LR sweep you're already running, plotted as a stability curve
  instead of collapsed to an argmax. Accuracy deltas on a saturated benchmark
  are fragile to tuning and contamination; a stability band is not.

  One trap to design around: DeLoRA's single scalar should also widen the band
  if normalization per se is what does it. So the LR-robustness result likely
  belongs to normalization, not to per-component scaling — and the per-component
  delta needs its own separate test. If you don't pre-register that split, the
  strongest result in the paper gets claimed for the wrong mechanism.

  On data — agreed, and going RL-first on verifiable rewards is cleaner than
  defending a 2023 SFT substrate. Two things it costs, which I've asked the
  agent to price honestly:

  - The dose axis loses its dense-CE endpoint. Built entirely inside RL (partial
  credit vs binary, G-sweep, verifier granularity) the axis is much narrower,
  and it may no longer power a slope. If so the marquee claim needs replacing —
  and LR-robustness is a candidate, since it's measurable inside RL too.
  - The thesis replication becomes mostly ceremonial. If the substrate changes,
  a "legacy anchor" run tests a protocol nobody will use again.

  Math vs code is a real fork: math gives a cheap, reliable verifier (answer
  matching) and no sandbox; code gives execution-based rewards, richer partial
  credit, and a better per-problem analysis unit, at the cost of a sandboxed
  executor and worse contamination exposure. I've asked for a specific
  recommendation with named datasets and sizes rather than "both."

  The agent is mid-flight and has the update. Its verification of the thesis
  claims against the PDF continues regardless.

✻ Brewed for 1m 11s

⏺ Agent "Adversarial review P1 and P5" finished · 14m 42s

⏺ The adversarial review verified P5's asymptotics — and then showed the
  conclusion I relayed to you doesn't follow. I need to correct what I told you.

  The math is right: KL ≈ κ²/(2m) at κ≪m, and Θ(m) at κ∝m, both confirmed
  numerically to 4–5 digits. But it inverted Davidson's own KL column (exact,
  since vMF KL depends on κ alone) and found the S-VAE never operates in that 
  regime: learned κ/m runs 4.6e5, 527, 82, 18.0, 4.85 at d = 2, 5, 10, 20, 40.
  At every dimension Davidson reports, κ ≫ m. Worse, KL at fixed κ is
  non-monotone in m, peaking at m* ≈ 0.76κ — and at the d=40 operating point
  (κ≈194, m*≈148) Davidson's entire sweep sits on the rising branch. So the
  plan's H2, "KL grows roughly linearly in m at fixed κ," is a serviceable
  description of the branch the data actually occupies. I told you the plan was
  mathematically wrong; it isn't, in the range that matters.

  Two related corrections to what I passed along: "informativeness requires κ =
  Θ(m)" is false — rate R needs only κ ≈ √(2mR), so a single scalar delivers any
  target rate at large m. And fixed-κ vMF is structurally immune to posterior
  collapse, because with κ fixed the rate term has zero gradient w.r.t. the
  encoder; only learned-κ is vulnerable. That's the configuration Xu & Durrett
  and Guu et al. actually used, so the blanket "not immune" was wrong in the
  direction that matters.

  The rate-allocation diagnosis also isn't novel — it's Davidson §5.1 verbatim
  ("its posterior becomes more expressive than the vMF due to the higher number
  of variance parameters") and already in the plan's §5.5.

  The one thing worth more than everything the proposal asserted, which it 
  didn't claim: Gate G1 requires "mean learned κ falling with d while KL still 
  rises." That conjunction is satisfiable only while m ≲ 0.76κ. Extrapolating
  Davidson's falling κ puts κ/m ≈ 1 near m ≈ 90–120, so at d ≥ 256 G1 is likely 
  mathematically unsatisfiable — it would fail for reasons unrelated to whether
  the science is right, after Tier 1 was already spent. Fix: restate G1 in terms
  of ρ = A_m(κ), or KL/m at fixed ρ, not raw KL.

  On P1, the linchpin didn't survive:

  - The rotation clamp is LARS. Computing s = min(1, τ‖w‖/‖Δw⊥‖) and scaling the
  update is a per-row adaptive step-size rule — the proposal concedes it by
  adding LARS as a sixth dose point. Off the sphere, weight rotation and step
  size are the same quantity up to the radial part, so the intervention can't
  separate "sphere" from "step size"; it is a step-size intervention with a
  geometric label.
  - It clamps the wrong object. The mediator is representation drift; the clamp
  bounds fan-in weight rotation, and the map between them is depth-compounding,
  data-dependent, and non-injective. The escape hatch — regress on realized
  angular motion — abandons the intervention and returns to the correlation it
  was meant to replace.
  - Cutting ResNet-50 defunds the plan's designated floor. It's the only source
  of p=2048 features, hence the only empirical instance of the ν=1023
  SciPy-underflow finding and "wider is less measurable" — which the plan names
  as what survives if everything else fails.
  - Your collider worry checks out as real, E7 is autocorrelation (early drift
  is a partial sum of the total), and E2's premise is contradicted by the
  literature — head-magnitude imbalance is precisely the thing known to compound
  with increment count (BiC, Weight Aligning).

  What survives on P1 is smaller and cheaper: extend the existing effective-LR
  match into a 5-point dose–response using weight decay (~2 GPU-h, not 24), add
  the LARS arm the prior review already costed at 0.2 GPU-h, and — the only
  design that could actually license a causal claim — a differentiable penalty
  on old-class feature displacement at matched step size, which intervenes on
  the real mediator and nobody proposed.

  Two agents still out.

✻ Cooked for 31s

⏺ Agent "Adversarial review merged P2 and P3" finished · 16m 53s

⏺ The P2/P3 review is in, and it's the most consequential of the three. Four
  things.

  Your LR point was right, and the reviewer withdrew the confound claim 
  outright. It checked every selected LR against its grid: LoRA/DoRA select 1e-4
  at r=2,4 and 5e-5 at r=8+ — interior to their grid at every rank on both
  models, with 5e-4 tested and rejected above. An interior argmax is evidence
  the grid bracketed the peak; a boundary argmax would have been evidence of
  starvation. Per-arm sweeping over arm-appropriate ranges is the correct
  protocol.

  And it endorses making the LR band the headline — over the accuracy delta. The
  argument that convinced me: the band estimand is definitionally immune to the
  undertuned-baseline attack, because it's referenced to each arm's own peak —
  a stronger baseline makes the comparison fairer, not the claim weaker. It also
  survives an accuracy null, which by the program's own priors is the modal
  outcome (~25% on Case A). That converts the likely result from a null paper
  into a positive one, which is the risk transformation the merge was reaching
  for and doesn't achieve. Estimand: band width in decades at pre-registered
  tolerance, plus divergence threshold. It flagged the confound you'd expect —
  s_i initializes at 0 and gates the whole update, so a near-zero-init learnable
  gain predicts a 100–200× tolerable LR with zero conditioning benefit — and
  the falsifier: re-express both bands in effective-step units
  (‖ΔW_{t+1}−ΔW_t‖_F/‖W₀‖_F, logged free). If they coincide there, it's
  reparameterization and the claim dies. Your per-component-vs-normalization
  worry gets an exact free null: at r=1 the two parameterizations are identical,
  so the measured band difference there must be zero and its spread is the
  noise floor.

  Theorem 1 in the thesis is false. Not untested — false. Its Eq. 8 assumes the
  û_i are mutually orthonormal, which nothing in the parameterization enforces.
  The reviewer checked numerically: for a rank-2 target with singular values
  (10,1), Theorem 1 predicts the global-scale variant has error 40.5; the actual
  minimum is 0.0 — exact fit, because γ·Σûᵢv̂ᵢᵀ with non-orthogonal unit factors
  realizes any spectrum with a+b ≤ 2. So Table 5's "spectrum constraint
  σ₁=…=σᵣ" is wrong, and the ~15 GPU-h experiment to test its prediction should
  be deleted rather than run. The empirical question that actually matters is
  the mutual coherence of the learned factors, which P3 already logs for free.

  It also sharpened BoolQ: 57.58 is below BoolQ's ~62.2% majority-class 
  baseline, and LoRA's own BoolQ in the same table recovers 57.58 → 63.67 →
  77.16 → 84.31 → 84.86 across ranks. So "the gain shrinks with rank" is
  arithmetically "LoRA's BoolQ recovers with rank." No per-seed numbers appear
  anywhere, so it's impossible to tell whether one seed collapsed.

  It rejects the merge, and I think correctly. Two objections I can't answer:

  - The proposed primary estimand was DeLoRA-PC − LoRA, which confounds
  normalization (already published, Bini et al.) with per-component scaling (the
  untested part). A positive slope on that contrast is a result belonging to
  DeLoRA's authors. The primary has to be DeLoRA-PC − DeLoRA.
  - The power claim is wrong three ways. The model includes (dose|seed) — a
  random slope — which does not drop out; effective n for the slope is 5 seeds, 
  not 25. The existing 2×2 already differences out seed-level terms under CRN,
  so the claimed variance trick isn't new. And "1.3 vs 1.6" compares a
  4-dose-unit contrast to a 2-unit one — the flat ~0.82 ratio across both ρ
  scenarios is the tell that it's a rescaling, not a variance calculation.

  On your data question: code, decisively. Partial credit only exists in code —
  the graded-reward axis is simply unavailable in math. And post-cutoff math
  sets are 15–30 problems (AIME/HMMT), which can't support the per-problem
  mixed-effects model the whole power argument rests on, versus LiveCodeBench's
  ~500–700 date-stamped problems. Math becomes a zero-training transfer check
  (~2–3 GPU-h) on the code-trained adapters. Train on ≤2025-05-31, evaluate
  ≥2025-06-01 — which fixes the leak and supplies the contamination design at
  once. Drop commonsense except for one ~8h run to test whether the BoolQ
  collapse reproduces.

  The counter-proposal to the merge is a split: Paper A = vision rank curve + LR
  band + r=1 null, ~110 GPU-h at MDE 0.58, standing alone under every outcome;
  Paper B = RL on code, gated on A. The power arithmetic behind that: reaching
  MDE 0.58 in the RL tier needs ~29 seeds ≈ 360 GPU-h for a single rank, versus
  43h for the entire 6-rank × 5-method vision grid. The merge demoted the
  highest-power tier to fund the lowest.

  One agent left (P4+P6).

✻ Crunched for 35s

⏺ Agent "Adversarial review P4 and P6" finished · 17m 48s

⏺ Last review is in, and it refutes two things I reported to you as findings.
  Both were numerically checked this time.

  The KappaFace audit target is dead. I relayed it as the audit's sharpest
  target. It's wrong twice over. Numerically: at p=512, n=70, a converged face
  class sits at κ/p ≈ 1–3, where relative RMSE is 1.9–2.9%, and Spearman
  correlation between true and estimated κ across classes is 0.995. The
  estimator works fine there. "75× below the boundary" describes n/p, but the
  signal is 20× above the null floor. And structurally it's a category error
  worse than I flagged: KappaFace z-scores κ̂ across classes before use, and a
  z-score removes any bias common to all classes identically. The critique
  cannot reach it even in principle.

  The √p / 64× claim is a bias-versus-standard-deviation comparison. Both
  estimators have null fluctuation of order √(p/n); p/√n is the mean of the
  estimated-mean null, which a matched null subtracts exactly. The honest number
  for detection is 0.93·p^(1/4) ≈ 7.4× at p=4096, not 64×. For estimation
  accuracy the gain is exactly zero — κ and μ are orthogonal parameters, so
  relative sd is identical. And it needs a μ known a priori: with a sample-split
  μ, power at p=4096, n=100 drops to 0.075 versus 0.110 for plain Rayleigh —
  worse than doing nothing.

  Three more that hit the flagship framing:

  - P6's central negative result is arithmetically false. "A converged CIFAR-100
  class at n=500 < p=512 is still unestimable" — measured RMSE is 0.63%, one of
  the best-conditioned cells in the program. At high concentration the bias
  floor is ≈1/n and doesn't involve p at all, so "n < p" carries no information
  once R̄ is large.
  - The bias shrinks gaps rather than inflating them. Simulated: true ratio 2.0
  between two groups reads as 1.267 at n=100. A common upward bias makes ordinal
  claims — which are most claims — conservative. And infeasibility lives
  entirely at κ/p ≤ 0.2, which is exactly where a paper's conclusion is
  "near-isotropic," so there the bias runs against the author's claim. The
  proposition "the bias direction matches the claim it supports" is true only in
  the regime where the bias is negligible.
  - Ethayarajh already ships a matched null — §3.4 defines and subtracts an
  anisotropy baseline, and the headline claims are ordinal. P4's assertion that
  these instruments have "no null distribution" is false for its own primary
  target.

  Realistic audit yield after the funnel: 0–2 consequential claims, ~10–25 "not
  resolvable as reported." That's an appendix table on a good methods paper, not
  a flagship.

  What survives, and it's still real:

  - P6 inverts into a better, constructive paper: κ̂ is well-conditioned
  precisely where the trace-ratio NC1's Σ_W Σ_B⁺ pseudo-inverse is singular
  (n_class < p). That's a positive claim rather than a demolition.
  - The noise-as-dose instrument (P6 E3) is the strongest single item in either 
  proposal and is currently buried — calibrated vMF dose with closed-form
  readout, per-layer angular amplification, adversarial perturbations expressed
  as noise-equivalent doses versus the CIFAR-10-C curve. No competing prior
  work, cheap, falsifiable either way, and it depends on none of the contested
  feasibility claims.
  - The tool is undersold: ive returns NaN at order 2047, so the admissibility
  formula itself is uncomputable in stock SciPy at p=4096. That's a better
  argument for shipping vmf-measure than the audit was.
  - A closed-form RMSE surface to replace the rectangular rule, matching
  simulation to 2 s.f.

  And one practical problem nobody had raised: three concurrent double-blind 
  submissions all version-pinning the same public artifact de-anonymizes the 
  group and tells any reviewer who checks that they're siblings — converting
  "three papers" into "one salami." The version-pin mechanism fixes code
  consistency, not credit, not schedule, and not this.

  Both rounds are done. Net picture across all six: the strongest surviving
  items are your LR-band reframe (P2), the noise-as-dose instrument (P6), the
  reward-granularity axis on code (P3), and the P4 instrument paper at honest
  scope — plus two real bugs caught before spend (the LiveCodeBench train/test
  leak, and P5's Gate G1 being mathematically unsatisfiable at d≥256).

  Want me to rewrite docs/research_program.md against all of this? It's a
  substantial revision — several headline claims come out, two projects change
  shape, and the merge gets replaced by the Paper A/Paper B split.

❯ It is not clear to me why we cannot/should not do the VAE experiment. I would 
  suggest we train a normalized architecture like a nGPT (or maybe better yet   
  nViT) with the vMF VAE encoding. If nothing else, then we can see where this

  single paper. A paper should be somewhat clean in terms of the story and this 
  would make it very muddy. Go through and clean up the proposals with these    
  comments and the reviews. The goal is to have at least 4-5 projects we can

  robustness). Based on the results, we can then go from there to determine any 
  potential conference submissions.                                             

⏺ Two things in your message change the frame, and the second one more than the
  first.

  On venue-first thinking: you're right to defer it. Every review I ran was
  optimizing "is this ICLR/CVPR," and that question distorted the designs — it's
  what produced the census flagship, the merge, and the multi-headline P6.
  Scoping for runnable and deciding venues from results is the correct order.

  On the nViT-VAE: nobody argued it can't be done — they argued against the
  text-VAE flagship, which is a different experiment. Your version is better
  than what was in the plan, and for a specific reason the adversarial review
  surfaced without noticing it applies here. Davidson's own confound is that the
  S-VAE decoder receives unit-norm inputs while the Gaussian VAE decoder
  receives inputs of norm √d — a factor-32 mismatch at d=1024. Every S-VAE
  result in the literature bolts a spherical latent onto an unnormalized
  encoder/decoder. Putting a vMF latent inside an architecture that is already
  hyperspherical removes that mismatch instead of controlling for it, and it's
  never been tried.

  Let me check what's committed before I rewrite.

           - singular values are non-uniform —

           -by a paper that never ran the                                
        29 -comparison. So P2's real question is not "does normalizing Lo
           -RA help" (answered, published) but                           
        30 -**"does per-component strength beat a single scalar, as the t
           -heory predicts?"** That is a sharper,                        
        31 -cheaper, more defensible paper, the baseline is a `pip instal
           -l` away, and it should be                                    
        32 -pre-registered as a diagnostics-and-rigor paper rather than a
           - method paper.

        64 +## Unresolved                                                
        65  
        86 -Sections 3–11 were drafted in parallel by separate agents aga
           -inst shared constraints and independent                      
        87 -literature briefs, then re-scoped twice as the compute envelo
           -pe firmed up. They occasionally disagree                     
        88 -with each other; section 11 catalogues where, and those disag
           -reements are left visible rather than

           -as not yet made. Where a project section

        91 -                                                             
        92 ----                                                          
        93 -                                                             
        94 -## The thesis, stated so it can be attacked                  
        95 -                                                             
        96 -> Constraining representations and weights to the unit sphere
           - removes the scale degree of freedom                         
        97 -> from optimization. Where the learning signal is dense and p
           -lentiful, this buys little — the                             
        98 -> network finds a good scale anyway. Where the signal is **sc
           -arce, noisy, or non-stationary**, the                        
        99 -> removed degree of freedom is one fewer thing to get wrong, 
           -and the constraint pays. Directional                         
       100 -> statistics is the right instrument to measure this, because
           - on the sphere the only remaining                            
       101 -> quantity *is* direction.                                   
       102 -                                                             
       103 -Three of the five projects are direct tests of the second sen
           -tence, in three different regimes of                         
       104 -scarcity:                                                    
       105 -                                                             
       106 -| Regime of scarcity | Project | Prediction |                
       107 -| --- | --- | --- |                                          
       108 -| Non-stationary signal — the task distribution changes under
           - you | P1, continual learning | Less forgetting, faster adapt
           -ation |                                                      
       109 -| Low-capacity signal — very few trainable parameters | P2, D
           -oLoRA at low rank | Gains grow as rank falls |               
       110 -| Sparse, high-variance signal — binary verifiable rewards | 
           -P3, RL on code | Gains exceed the SFT gains, and seed varianc
           -e falls |                                                    
       111 -| Adversarially perturbed signal | P6, robustness | Angular p
           -erturbation is the right threat geometry; normalized nets res
           -ist it better |                                              
       112 -                                                             
       113 -The remaining projects supply the instrument (P4) and test th
           -e same geometry in a generative                              
       114 -setting where the sphere is a modelling choice rather than an
           - optimization one (P5).                                      
       115 -                                                             
       116 -P6 also carries the program's most favorable measurement regi
           -me. Elsewhere κ̂ is fighting the                              
       117 -feasibility limits below; in a trained classifier, within-cla
           -ss latents are *highly* concentrated —                       
       118 -that is exactly what neural collapse NC1 asserts — so κ is la
           -rge, κ/p is comfortable, and the                             
       119 -estimator works as intended. That makes P6 both a robustness 
           -paper and the cleanest demonstration                         
       120 -that the instrument measures something real. The connection i
           -s worth stating plainly: **NC1 is                            
       121 -conventionally a ratio of within- to between-class covariance
           - traces; on normalized features the                          
       122 -principled directional version of within-class variability is
           - the vMF concentration.** κ̂ is a                             
       123 -better-specified NC1 metric — dimension-comparable, likelihoo
           -d-based, and equipped with a null.                           
       124 -                                                             
       125 -**The honest caveat, up front.** This thesis has a well-known
           - competing explanation: normalizing                          
       126 -weights changes the *effective learning rate*, and a good dea
           -l of what looks like "geometry helps"                        
       127 -in the literature has turned out to be "you accidentally tune
           -d the step size better." That confound                       
       128 -threatens P1, P2, and P3 simultaneously. It is the single mos
           -t important thing this program must                          
       129 -control for, and the reason section 7 specifies one shared pr
           -otocol for it rather than leaving each                       
       130 -project to improvise. If the effect does not survive matched-
           -effective-LR controls, the program's                         
       131 -thesis is wrong, and finding that out cheaply is worth more t
           -han three papers built on sand.                              
       132 -                                                             
       133 ----                                                          
       134 -                                                             
       135 -## Where this starts from                                    
       136 -                                                             
       137 -Two unpublished positive results and one finished piece of in
           -frastructure.                                                
       138 -                                                             
       139 -### Seed 1 — continual learning (needs replication; code not 
           -retained)                                                    
       140 -                                                             
       141 -An MLP trained on 5 of CIFAR-10's classes to convergence, the
           -n its head extended by 5 rows and                            
       142 -trained on the 5 held-out classes with no rehearsal. With nGP
           -T-style normalized MLPs the model                            
       143 -converged **faster on the new classes** *and* **forgot less o
           -n the old ones** — which is notable                          
       144 -because those usually trade off against each other. A 3D PCA 
           -projection showed the normalized                             
       145 -model's embeddings barely moving during phase 2 while the unn
           -ormalized model twisted the whole                            
       146 -space.                                                       
       147 -                                                             
       148 -That last observation is the seed of the whole program's meas
           -urement story, and it is currently a                         
       149 -picture. Turning it into a number with a null is P1's methodo
           -logical contribution and P4's reason                         
       150 -to exist.                                                    
       151 -                                                             
       152 -### Seed 2 — DoLoRA (needs replication; code not retained)   
       153 -                                                             
       154 -`papers/nLoRA.pdf`, an anonymous ICLR 2026 submission, normal
           -izes LoRA's rank-1 factors directly                          
       155 -and learns per-component non-negative scales:                
       156 -                                                             
       157 -```                                                          
       158 -ΔW = (α/r) · Σᵢ sᵢ · ûᵢ v̂ᵢᵀ ,   ‖ûᵢ‖ = ‖v̂ᵢ‖ = 1 ,   sᵢ = soft
           -plus(λᵢ) − log 2 ,   λᵢ init 0                               
       159 -```                                                          
       160 -                                                             
       161 -Reported gains are largest at low rank — LLaMA-3-8B at r=2 go
           -es from 75.42 to 83.05 average, GSM8K                        
       162 -from 53.5 to 61.0 — and narrow to about +1 by r=32. The group
           - reproduced better convergence and                           
       163 -accuracy on SFT.                                             
       164 -                                                             
       165 -Two details make this more than a PEFT trick. First, its grad
           -ient                                                         
       166 -`∇_{bᵢ} ∝ (sᵢ/‖bᵢ‖)(I − b̂ᵢb̂ᵢᵀ)(∇_W L) âᵢ` is an **orthogonal 
           -projection onto the tangent space of                         
       167 -the unit-norm constraint** — the same Riemannian retraction s
           -tructure as nGPT's block update, which                       
       168 -is what makes P2 and P3 part of this program rather than a de
           -tour. Second, the paper's own stated                         
       169 -limitations are precisely two of the proposed projects: *"oth
           -er modalities, such as Vision                                
       170 -Transformers"* and *"the interaction between component-level 
           -decomposition and specialized training                       
       171 -objectives like RLHF remains an area for future work."*      
       172 -                                                             
       173 -### Infrastructure already built                             
       174 -                                                             
       175 -- **Fast batched vMF sampling** — 126.97× (NumPy) and 99.01× 
           -(Torch) over the SciPy reference at                          
       176 -  d=4096, single Householder reflection, device-native, fp16 
           -through fp64.                                                
       177 -- **Batched κ estimators** — Banerjee, Sra's two-step, safegu
           -arded Newton, against compiled CUSF                          
       178 -  log-Bessel; 5.3M estimates/s at batch 2²⁰.                 
       179 -- **A characterized estimator**, which is what makes it usabl
           -e rather than merely fast. See below.                        
       180 -                                                             
       181 ----                                                          
       182 -                                                             
       183 -## The constraint that shapes every measurement in this progr
           -am                                                           
       184 -                                                             
       185 -The κ benchmark (`docs/kappa_report.html`) established three 
           -facts that bound what the measurement                        
       186 -projects can claim. They are repeated in each section because
           - ignoring them produces confident                            
       187 -nonsense.                                                    
       188 -                                                             
       189 -**SciPy's Bessel path is unusable at p ≥ 1024.** `scipy.speci
           -al.ive` underflows to exactly zero                           
       190 -above order ≈511. Sra-with-SciPy returns NaN; safeguarded New
           -ton *silently* stalls at ~1e-3                               
       191 -relative error — the dangerous failure, because it returns a 
           -plausible number. Every dimension this                       
       192 -program cares about (768, 1024, 4096) is past that line, so C
           -USF or the PyTorch port is mandatory,                        
       193 -not preferred.                                               
       194 -                                                             
       195 -**κ/p governs accuracy, not p.** Above κ/p ≈ 0.2, roughly n =
           - 10–25p samples buy 1% accuracy. Below                       
       196 -κ/p ≈ 0.05, even n = 10⁵ does not. A high-dimensional but con
           -centrated fit is easy; a                                     
       197 -low-dimensional diffuse one is hard.                         
       198 -                                                             
       199 -**The finite-sample bias is systematically upward, and it poi
           -nts the same way as the claim.**                             
       200 -Anyone fitting a vMF to representations wants to conclude "mo
           -re concentrated than uniform." That is                       
       201 -exactly the direction the artifact pushes. So a **matched nul
           -l** — n uniform unit vectors in ℝᵖ,                          
       202 -same estimator, same n, whose mean is *not* zero — is not goo
           -d practice, it is the only thing                             
       203 -separating the measurement from the artifact.                
       204 -                                                             
       205 -Practical consequence for this program: κ̂ on a handful of vec
           -tors is hopeless. Any proposed                               
       206 -measurement must state its (n, p, κ) regime and show it lands
           - in the estimable region, or use a                           
       207 -different instrument. Where κ̂ does not apply, CKA, Procrustes
           - disparity, and subspace principal                           
       208 -angles do, and every geometric claim in this program should r
           -est on more than one of them.                                
       209 -                                                             
       210 ----                                                          
       211 -                                                             
       212 -## The six projects at a glance                              
       213 -                                                             
       214 -| # | Project | Venue | What is new | Biggest risk |         
       215 -| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |                              
       216 -| P1 | Normalized networks for continual learning | CVPR | Fu
           -ll hyperspherical architecture + quantified drift, beyond kno
           -wn cosine-classifier results | Prior art in cosine classifier
           -s; effective-LR confound |                                   
       217 -| P2 | DoLoRA replication and extension to vision | ICLR | Re
           -implementation, mechanism diagnosis, the modality gap the pap
           -er names | The +7.63 at r=2 may not reproduce |              
       218 -| P3 | Normalized adapters for RL on code generation | ICLR |
           - Tests the paper's own stated future work; sparse-reward regi
           -me | RL variance may swallow the effect |                    
       219 -| P4 | Directional statistics as a measurement instrument | I
           -CLR | Bias-corrected κ̂ in the LLM regime; null tables; audit 
           -of the isotropy literature | A measurement paper needs a surp
           -rising finding |                                             
       220 -| P5 | Hyperspherical latent models at scale | ICLR | Breaks 
           -the m>40 wall; honest vMF vs Power Spherical | May confirm th
           -e competitor rather than beat it |                           
       221 -| P6 | Classifier latents and adversarial robustness on the s
           -phere | CVPR | κ̂ as a directional NC1 metric; vMF noise at ma
           -tched angular displacement; angular certificates | Gradient m
           -asking could fake the robustness result |                    
       222 -                                                             
       223 -P4 is the instrument the others use. P1 is the flagship becau
           -se it already has a positive                                 
       224 -preliminary result; P6 is the safest bet because its measurem
           -ent half is nearly guaranteed to                             
       225 -produce something publishable even if the robustness half fai
           -ls. A defensible sequencing argument                         
       226 -is in section 7, and section 8 argues with all of this.      
       227 -                                                             
       228 -**One warning that belongs at the top.** P6's robustness clai
           -ms and P1's stability claims are both                        
       229 -vulnerable to a defense that is not real: normalization can b
           -reak gradient-based attacks without                          
       230 -conferring robustness (obfuscated gradients), just as it can 
           -improve continual learning by                                
       231 -accidentally rescaling the learning rate. Both projects must 
           -run their respective control suites —                        
       232 -transfer and black-box attacks for P6, matched-effective-LR f
           -or P1 — before any claim is written                          
       233 -down. A result that survives those controls is worth far more
           - than three that do not.

       237 -                                                             
       238 -## Hardware and model envelope                               
       239 -                                                             
       240 -**This section is authoritative and supersedes any hardware o
           -r model assumption made in sections                          
       241 -2–7.** Those sections were drafted across several revisions o
           -f the budget; where one of them names a                      
       242 -different GPU, a different model, or a stale cost, this secti
           -on wins.                                                     
       243 -                                                             
       244 -### Compute                                                  
       245 -                                                             
       246 -| Tier | Hardware | Rule |                                   
       247 -| --- | --- | --- |                                          
       248 -| Default | 1× RTX 3090 (24 GB) or 1× A6000 (48 GB) | ≤1000 G
           -PU-h per project; target the lowest decisive tier |          
       249 -| Scale-up | 2× A100 | Available, but deliberately **later** 
           -— to demonstrate scale once an effect is established, not for
           - exploration |                                               
       250 -| Earned | A few thousand H100-h | High-belief projects only;
           - see the escalation ladder |                                 
       251 -                                                             
       252 -The distinction that matters is **pre-training versus post-tr
           -aining**, not raw model size.                                
       253 -                                                             
       254 -- **Pre-training from scratch:** stay small, ≤~100M parameter
           -s — nanoGPT/nViT/nMLP ladders. The one                       
       255 -  correction worth stating loudly: **ImageNet-1k from scratch
           - is affordable** with a fast data                            
       256 -  pipeline. FFCV reaches 75% top-1 with ResNet-50 in ~2.7 A10
           -0-h (≈6–8 A6000-h per run), and                              
       257 -  ResNet-18 at ~3× the throughput is ~2–3 A6000-h per run. It
           - requires preprocessing to FFCV format                       
       258 -  on fast local NVMe (~150–300 GB); measure images/sec on one
           - epoch before committing. ViT from                           
       259 -  scratch on ImageNet remains out (~300–500 A100-h/run for De
           -iT-S).                                                       
       260 -- **Post-training** (SFT, RL, PEFT, inference): bounded by 48
           - GB, and **LoRA makes that generous**,                       
       261 -  since the frozen base carries no gradients and no optimizer
           - states. 8B LoRA bf16 ≈ 20–24 GB;                            
       262 -  14B ≈ 32–36 GB; 32B via QLoRA (NF4) ≈ 18 GB. Full finetunin
           -g caps near 1.5B. For RL, the reference                      
       263 -  policy is free — disable the adapter rather than holding a 
           -second copy (~16 GB saved at 8B).                            
       264 -                                                             
       265 -**Consequence: VRAM is rarely the binding constraint. Wall-cl
           -ock is.** Cost every experiment in                           
       266 -hours, not gigabytes.                                        
       267 -                                                             
       268 -### Models                                                   
       269 -                                                             
       270 -Anything **trained or finetuned** uses the **Qwen3.5** family
           - (Apache 2.0): **4B, 9B, and 27B dense**                     
       271 -plus a **35B MoE**. Do not use older Qwen generations.       
       272 -                                                             
       273 -This applies to training, not to analysis. Projects that *mea
           -sure* released checkpoints (P4's                             
       274 -embedding atlas, P6's deferred LM study) should keep generati
           -onal and architectural diversity on                          
       275 -purpose — Pythia's ~150-checkpoint training trajectory in par
           -ticular has no modern replacement and is                     
       276 -the only way to measure how geometry evolves over training.  
       277 -                                                             
       278 -### A note on scope discipline                               
       279 -                                                             
       280 -The compute planning in this document went through three revi
           -sions and consumed more attention than                       
       281 -it deserves. It is now settled; the numbers below are good en
           -ough to start on. The gates matter more                      
       282 -than the estimates — every project's Tier 0 is a handful of G
           -PU-hours, and running one of them will                       
       283 -teach more than another round of costing. **Other promising d
           -irections exist outside this program and                     
       284 -should not be crowded out by it.**                           
       285 -                                                             
       286 -                                                             
       287 ----                                                          
       288 -                                                             
       289 -## Project 1 — Hyperspherical Networks for Class-Incremental 
           -Learning: Architecture-Wide Normalization and a Null-Calibrat
           -ed Measurement of Representation Drift                       
       290 -                                                             
       291 -> **COMPUTE ENVELOPE (binding).** Total re-scoped budget: **≈
           - 400 RTX-A6000-hours** (48 GB), across **≈ 1,480 training run
           -s**. Hard ceiling 1,000 GPU-h. On a single RTX 3090 (24 GB) t
           -he same program costs **≈ 490 GPU-hours** — still under the c
           -eiling, but with two arms (ResNet-50/ImageNet at batch 256, a
           -nd any full ViT-B finetune) pushed to the edge of or past 24 
           -GB; see §1.7.3. This supersedes a previous re-scope that came
           - in at 98 3090-hours by **deleting ImageNet-1k and the entire
           - pretrained-model tier**. That cut was wrong. It assumed 90-e
           -poch torchvision recipes and network-storage JPEG decoding, a
           -nd on those assumptions ImageNet is indeed unaffordable. With
           - **FFCV** (or DALI/WebDataset) plus a modern short-schedule r
           -ecipe, class-incremental ImageNet-1k with ResNet-18/50 costs 
           -~214 GPU-h for the whole study, and the pretrained-ViT tier —
           - whose per-run cost was never the problem — costs ~89 GPU-h o
           -nce its *breadth* is trimmed from 9 baselines × 2 backbones ×
           - 3 benchmarks × 5 seeds to 5 baselines × 1 backbone × 2 bench
           -marks × 3–5 seeds. Both are back in scope. The venue target i
           -s **CVPR main track again** (§ "Venue position").            
       292 -                                                             
       293 -**Claim.** Constraining *every* representation and *every* we
           -ight row of a classifier to the unit sphere (nGPT-style: norm
           -alized hidden states, normalized weight rows, learnable per-c
           -oordinate residual "eigen learning rates" α, cosine output he
           -ad with learnable logit scale) improves the **stability–plast
           -icity frontier** in rehearsal-free class-incremental learning
           - (CIL) — not merely the endpoint — and it does so because it 
           -suppresses **coherent representation drift**: old-class featu
           -res rotate less, and what rotation remains is closer to a rig
           -id, label-preserving motion.                                 
       294 -                                                             
       295 -**Why it should be true.** Forgetting in CIL is largely (i) o
           -utput-head magnitude/bias imbalance between old and new class
           -es (LUCIR, CVPR 2019; BiC, CVPR 2019; Weight Aligning, CVPR 2
           -020) and (ii) drift of old-class prototypes in feature space 
           -(SDC 2004.00440; LDC 2407.08536), with the backbone degrading
           - far less than task accuracy suggests (Davari et al., 2203.13
           -381). Cosine heads fix (i) by construction. Full-network norm
           -alization additionally removes the uncontrolled weight/activa
           -tion magnitude growth that Karras et al. (EDM2, 2312.02696) i
           -dentify as the actual pathology, and pins each layer's *angul
           -ar* update size to an equilibrium (Kosson, 2305.17212; Wan, 2
           -006.08419) so that phase-2 gradients cannot silently acquire 
           -a large effective step on the phase-1 solution. The mechanism
           - is therefore "bounded angular motion per step" — a *measurab
           -le* prediction, not just an accuracy claim.                  
       296 -                                                             
       297 -**Pitch (one sentence).** We take nGPT's hyperspherical archi
           -tecture out of language modeling and into class-incremental l
           -earning, evaluate it from CIFAR-10 MLPs all the way to from-s
           -cratch ImageNet-1k and the pretrained-ViT SOTA cluster, sweep
           - every confound rather than arguing it away, and replace the 
           -field's eyeball-level "the representation moved less" claim w
           -ith a null-calibrated von Mises–Fisher concentration measurem
           -ent of per-sample angular drift, validated in the (n, p) regi
           -me where that estimator is trustworthy.                      
       298 -                                                             
       299 -**Honest prior-art position — read before writing the intro.*
           -* "Cosine/normalized classifiers reduce forgetting" is **alre
           -ady published**, repeatedly: Gidaris & Komodakis (CVPR 2018),
           - Qi et al. imprinted weights (CVPR 2018), LUCIR (CVPR 2019), 
           -PODNet (ECCV 2020), Lesort et al.'s last-layer study (2106.01
           -834). "Fixed hyperspherical/simplex-ETF prototypes reduce for
           -getting" is also published (NC-FSCIL, ICLR 2023, 2302.03004; 
           -ProNC, 2505.24254; NCCL, 2412.02865; hyperspherical prototype
           -s, Mettes et al., NeurIPS 2019). "vMF distributions in contin
           -ual learning" is published: **Michel et al., AAAI 2024** (*Le
           -arning Representations on the Unit Sphere*) fit a mixture of 
           -vMF with per-class prototype and concentration for *online* C
           -L — cite and differentiate **on page 1**. "Hyperspherical nor
           -malization preserves plasticity" is published on the RL side 
           -(NaP, 2407.01800; SimbaV2, 2502.15280). The defensible novelt
           -y budget is exactly two items: **(a)** architecture-wide nGPT
           - normalization (weights + hidden states + eigen-LRs, LayerNor
           -m removed) in CIL — nobody has done nGPT-in-CL, and it is *no
           -t* the same as a cosine classifier; **(b)** treating the geom
           -etry claim as a **measurement problem** with a matched null, 
           -a stated feasibility regime, and numerically valid log-Bessel
           - at p ≥ 1024 (and now p = 2048, where the problem is worse). 
           -Item (b) is the safer contribution and should carry the paper
           - if (a) shrinks under ablation. The scale (ImageNet-1k + PTM 
           -SOTA) is not itself a contribution; it is the *admission tick
           -et*, and the paper must not pretend otherwise.               
       300 -                                                             
       301 -**Venue position: CVPR main track, and here is the argument.*
           -* Three things a CVPR reviewer in this area demands, and how 
           -the restored plan supplies each.                             
       302 -1. **From-scratch large-scale CIL.** Split-ImageNet-1k with R
           -esNet-18 *and* ResNet-50, 10×100 B0 plus the B500+5×100 large
           --base convention that the LUCIR/PODNet/iCaRL ImageNet tables 
           -actually use, 2 orders × 3 seeds (§1.4, Tier 3). This is the 
           -axis whose absence forced the previous downgrade.            
       303 -2. **Comparison against the current SOTA cluster.** Split-Ima
           -geNet-R 10×20 and PTM Split-CIFAR-100 10×10 with a pretrained
           - ViT-B/16 against **L2P, DualPrompt, CODA-Prompt, RanPAC, EAS
           -E** plus a **SimpleCIL** frozen-feature control, 5 seeds (§1.
           -5, Tier 4). These are run at their **published full schedules
           -** — they are cheap — so the SOTA comparison is at full fidel
           -ity even though the from-scratch ImageNet arm is not.        
       304 -3. **A contribution that is not "we tried X and it went up."*
           -* The null-calibrated directional-drift measurement, the κ/p 
           -feasibility map, the SciPy `ive` underflow finding, and the n
           - < p per-class collapse (§1.2). This is what distinguishes th
           -e paper from the twelfth normalization-helps-forgetting submi
           -ssion.                                                       
       305 -                                                             
       306 -The one honest asterisk: the from-scratch ImageNet numbers us
           -e short schedules (16–32 epochs/task-equivalent, ResNet-18 ≈ 
           -67–71% joint top-1 rather than 76%). Those numbers support **
           -relative** comparisons between arms, all trained under an ide
           -ntical budget, and the paper must say so in the caption of ev
           -ery ImageNet table. Absolute-accuracy SOTA on from-scratch Im
           -ageNet CIL is explicitly **not** claimed and is listed as def
           -erred (D3). A CVPR reviewer will accept a budget-matched rela
           -tive comparison that is stated as such; they will not accept 
           -a budget-matched comparison dressed up as SOTA.
