      2627 -                                                             
      2628 -## 1. P6 — novelty and validity audit                        
      2629 -                                                             
      2630 -### 1.1 Is "κ̂ as a better-specified NC1" novel?              
      2631 -                                                             
      2632 -**No, not as stated.** §6.1's prior-art paragraph is honest a
           -s far as it goes and correctly names                         
      2633 -CIDER, KappaFace, NECO and VCI. It misses the closest hits, a
           -nd the ones it misses are the ones a                         
      2634 -reviewer finds first:                                        
      2635 -                                                             
      2636 -- **SIREN (Du et al., NeurIPS 2022, "Shaping Representations 
           -for Detecting Out-of-Distribution                            
      2637 -  Objects")** models class-conditional penultimate features a
           -s a **mixture of von Mises–Fisher                            
      2638 -  distributions with per-class concentration**, and its test-
           -time score is a **parametric vMF                             
      2639 -  likelihood** under those fitted distributions. That is §6.1
           -'s estimand and §6.3's per-input score,                      
      2640 -  published four years ago. It is not cited anywhere in 065. 
           -This is the single most damaging                             
      2641 -  omission in the section.                                   
      2642 -- **HUG / Generalized Neural Collapse (arXiv:2303.06484)** al
           -ready replaces the trace-ratio NC1 with                      
      2643 -  a *directional* intra-class-variability measure on the hype
           -rsphere and argues explicitly that this                      
      2644 -  is the right generalization when d < C−1. P4 §4.1 cites it;
           - P6 does not, even though P6 is the                          
      2645 -  section making the NC1 claim.                              
      2646 -- **vMF-mixture interpretations of normalized softmax** (Hasn
           -at et al. 2017; NormFace, which P1 §1.1                      
      2647 -  cites) already establish that a cosine classifier *is* a sh
           -ared-κ mixture-of-vMF likelihood. So                         
      2648 -  "on normalized features the principled directional form of 
           -within-class variability is the vMF                          
      2649 -  concentration" is a restatement, not a discovery.          
      2650 -- **Neural collapse under adversarial perturbation and in adv
           -ersarially trained networks** is                             
      2651 -  already a paper (the "robustness of neural collapse / neura
           -l collapse of robustness" line): NC                          
      2652 -  metrics decrease on both clean and perturbed points under A
           -T, and NC1 collapse is weaker on                             
      2653 -  perturbed points than on clean ones. That is the qualitativ
           -e result §6.6 would report. Cite it and                      
      2654 -  state the delta, which is the null calibration, not the phe
           -nomenon.                                                     
      2655 -                                                             
      2656 -**What is actually unclaimed, and it is worth having:**      
      2657 -                                                             
      2658 -1. The **admissibility boundary** — the operating rule `R̄ > 0
           -.19` / `0.05 < R̄ ≤ 0.19` / `R̄ ≤ 0.05`                        
      2659 -   with a matched null in every cell, and the demonstration t
           -hat per-class NC1-as-κ is *unmeasurable*                     
      2660 -   on exactly the benchmarks where the NC literature reports 
           -it most confidently (CIFAR-100,                              
      2661 -   Tiny-ImageNet, wide features, ~500 images/class).         
      2662 -2. The **`p`-sweep at fixed backbone and fixed n** (T1.4). Th
           -is is the direct test of "κ/p governs                        
      2663 -   accuracy, not p" and it is the single most defensible new 
           -measurement in the whole program. It                         
      2664 -   costs 7 GPU-h and it should be the section's Figure 1.    
      2665 -3. The **superclass-pooling + narrow-projection-head fix**, w
           -hich turns the negative result into a                        
      2666 -   constructive one.                                         
      2667 -                                                             
      2668 -**Verdict.** Items 1–3 are a real contribution. They are a **
           -measurement/methods paper — TMLR or an                       
      2669 -ICLR methods slot — not CVPR.** §6.1's own self-assessment ("
           -the metric framing alone is thin") is                        
      2670 -correct and should be believed. The CVPR framing depends enti
           -rely on the robustness half, and the                         
      2671 -robustness half is the weak half. Recommend: pre-register the
           - venue decision as a function of the                         
      2672 -Tier-0 outcome, rather than assuming CVPR in 075 and in the o
           -verview.                                                     
      2673 -                                                             
      2674 -**One thing the section should add and does not, because it w
           -ould be the paper's teeth.** §6.2                            
      2675 -demonstrates that per-class κ is unmeasurable at CIFAR-100 sc
           -ale. It never *audits an existing                            
      2676 -claim*. P4 §4.4(e) does exactly this for the isotropy literat
           -ure. The analogue here is nearly free:                       
      2677 -take 10–20 released CIFAR-100 / Tiny-ImageNet checkpoints (ti
           -mm, RobustBench), recompute the                              
      2678 -published NC1 numbers, put a matched null next to each, and p
           -roduce a table of which published NC1                        
      2679 -claims survive. That is ~1 GPU-h and it converts "here is a b
           -oundary" into "here are the published                        
      2680 -numbers that fall on the wrong side of it." Without it, §6.2 
           -is a rule nobody has to obey.                                
      2681 -                                                             
      2682 -### 1.2 Is the adversarial-detection half credible against mo
           -dern baselines?                                              
      2683 -                                                             
      2684 -**No, as currently specified.** Three problems, in descending
           - order.                                                      
      2685 -                                                             
      2686 -**(a) The baselines are OOD-detection baselines, not adversar
           -ial-detection baselines.** MSP (2016),                       
      2687 -ODIN (2017), Mahalanobis (2018) and energy (2020) are the sta
           -ndard *out-of-distribution* suite.                           
      2688 -Adversarial-example detection is a different and much more ho
           -stile literature, and the reference                          
      2689 -points a reviewer will name are **Carlini & Wagner 2017 ("Adv
           -ersarial examples are not easily                             
      2690 -detected: bypassing ten detection methods")** and **Tramèr, I
           -CML 2022 ("Detecting adversarial                             
      2691 -examples is (nearly) as hard as classifying them")**. Tramèr'
           -s reduction is the fatal one: a robust                       
      2692 -detector at distance ε implies a robust classifier at ε/2, an
           -d he revisits 13 detector defenses and                       
      2693 -shows that in 11 of them the claimed detection results would 
           -imply classifiers far beyond SOTA.                           
      2694 -Neither paper appears in §6.3. Any reviewer who works in this
           - area will open with them, and "we beat                      
      2695 -Mahalanobis on AUROC" is not a response.                     
      2696 -                                                             
      2697 -To be fair, §6.3 does the two things that matter most: it run
           -s an **adaptive attack** (100-step PGD                       
      2698 -on `L_CE − λ log p_vMF`, 5 λ values) and it states plainly th
           -at the per-input score is cosine-to-                         
      2699 -class-mean with a calibration wrapper. That honesty is worth 
           -a lot. But the framing has to change:                        
      2700 -this is not "does κ detect adversarial inputs", it is "at mat
           -ched cost, and in the `n_class < p`                          
      2701 -regime where Mahalanobis' class-conditional covariance is sin
           -gular, a directional score is                                
      2702 -well-posed where the covariance score is not." That is an **e
           -stimation-well-posedness** result, and                       
      2703 -§6.3 already says so in its efficiency paragraph. Lead with i
           -t, cite Tramèr up front as the reason                        
      2704 -the paper does *not* claim a security result, and the section
           - becomes defensible.                                         
      2705 -                                                             
      2706 -**(b) There is no distribution-shift control.** If the vMF li
           -kelihood fires on adversarial inputs, a                      
      2707 -reviewer will immediately ask whether it fires on **CIFAR-10-
           -C** at matched accuracy degradation. A                       
      2708 -detector that cannot distinguish an L∞ adversarial perturbati
           -on from Gaussian noise or JPEG                               
      2709 -compression is a shift detector, not an adversarial detector.
           - CIFAR-10-C is a download and the score                      
      2710 -is inference-only: this costs under 0.5 GPU-h and its absence
           - is the most predictable reviewer                            
      2711 -demand in the section.                                       
      2712 -                                                             
      2713 -**(c) There is no held-out attack.** All four attacks in T1.5
           - are in the design set. A detector                           
      2714 -tuned — even implicitly, via layer-aggregation choices — on F
           -GSM/PGD/C&W/AutoAttack must be shown to                      
      2715 -transfer to an attack it never saw. Free, since the attacks a
           -re already implemented.                                      
      2716 -                                                             
      2717 -**Power note on §6.3.** G1(e)'s threshold (vMF AUROC ≥ Mahala
           -nobis − 0.02 on ≥3/4 attacks) is the                         
      2718 -one detection criterion that is adequately powered on the *ev
           -aluation* axis — with 1,000 images the                       
      2719 -paired AUROC-difference SE is ~0.005, so 0.02 is ~4 SE. It is
           - **not** powered on the training-seed                        
      2720 -axis: T1.5 runs on "4 models" with no seed replication, so a 
           -0.02 AUROC difference attributable to                        
      2721 -which seed trained the encoder is invisible. Say so, or share
           - encoders across the comparison so the                       
      2722 -contrast is within-encoder.                                  
      2723 -                                                             
      2724 -### 1.3 Are P6's gates real?                                 
      2725 -                                                             
      2726 -Mostly yes, and in two places they are the most rigorous gate
           -s in the program — the Tier-0 masking                        
      2727 -pre-screen with numeric thresholds fixed in advance, the blac
           -k-box Square-500 diagnostic in the                           
      2728 -*screen* rather than only in the Tier-2 checklist, the day-3 
           -tripwire on (d1)/(d4), the standing                          
      2729 -publication rule on sub-0.25-L2 radii, and the cheapest-first
           - ordering of G2a → G2b → G2c. That is                        
      2730 -genuinely good design and it should not be watered down. But 
           -four specific defects:                                       
      2731 -                                                             
      2732 -**G0(a) is vacuous.** It requires penultimate-layer `R̄` on RN
           --18/CIFAR-10 train to cross **0.19**                         
      2733 -before end of training. At TPT with L2-normalized features, w
           -ithin-class mean pairwise cosine on a                        
      2734 -converged ResNet-18/CIFAR-10 is routinely 0.7–0.9, giving R̄ ≈
           - 0.84–0.95. This passes with                                 
      2735 -near-certainty. The second clause ("exceeds the matched null 
           -by ≥ 10 null SDs") is subsumed by the                        
      2736 -first by a factor of ~40: sd(R̄_null) ≈ 1/√(2np) = 4.4e−4 at n
           - = 5000, p = 512, so R̄ = 0.19 is                             
      2737 -already ~400 null SDs above the null mean of 0.014. The gate 
           -also does not say whether R̄ is                               
      2738 -per-class or pooled — and pooled R̄ measures the common-direct
           -ion/cone effect (NC2-adjacent), not                          
      2739 -NC1. Specify per-class, and set a threshold that can fail.   
      2740 -                                                             
      2741 -**G0(b) is arithmetically impossible as written and is comput
           -ed on 10 points.** Spearman across the                       
      2742 -**10 CIFAR-10 classes**. The gate demands |ρ| ≥ 0.5 *and* per
           -mutation p < 0.05; at n = 10 the 5%                          
      2743 -two-sided critical value is |ρ| ≈ 0.648, so the stated ρ thre
           -shold is inoperative and the real                            
      2744 -threshold is 0.648 — which the section never says. Worse, thi
           -s is one of four *conjunctive*                               
      2745 -conditions that can kill the project at 9.9 h. Fix: run the d
           -ifficulty correlation on **CIFAR-100's                       
      2746 -100 classes** (T1.1), where n = 100 gives real power, and dem
           -ote the CIFAR-10 version to descriptive.                     
      2747 -                                                             
      2748 -**G0(c) gates the robustness half on a quantity that is eithe
           -r zero or an artifact.** It requires the                     
      2749 -normalized-vs-standard difference in T0.3 to survive T0.4's c
           -onfirmation at "~0.9 points clean,                           
      2750 -~1.6–2.5 points robust". T0.3 trains **standard, non-adversar
           -ial** ResNet-18s for 200 epochs. Robust                      
      2751 -accuracy of such models under PGD at ε = 8/255 is 0.0% for bo
           -th arms. So either the gate collapses                        
      2752 -to a clean-accuracy comparison — which is not evidence about 
           -robustness at all — or the normalized                        
      2753 -arm shows non-zero robust accuracy without AT, which is exact
           -ly the gradient-masking signature that                       
      2754 -G0(d) is built to detect. **G0(c) and G0(d) are in direct ten
           -sion.** The fix is the one in the                            
      2755 -"Read this first" list: hoist a small Fast-AT block into Tier
           - 0 and gate on that.                                         
      2756 -                                                             
      2757 -**The conjunction structure gives a high false-stop rate.** G
           -0 requires (a) AND (b) AND (c) AND (d),                      
      2758 -and (d) is itself a five-way conjunction of noisy diagnostics
           - evaluated in ≥2/3 seeds. If each of the                     
      2759 -five masking diagnostics has even a 10% chance of a spurious 
           -fail, P(all five pass) ≈ 0.59; fold in                       
      2760 -(b) at n = 10 and the project has a substantial chance of bei
           -ng killed at 9.9 h by noise rather than                      
      2761 -by evidence. Gates that fail cheaply are the right design, bu
           -t a gate that fails *wrongly* and                            
      2762 -cheaply is worse than no gate. Recommend: make (d) the blocki
           -ng condition — it is the one with a real                     
      2763 -decision attached — and make (a)/(b) reportable diagnostics t
           -hat inform scope rather than kill it.                        
      2764 -                                                             
      2765 -### 1.4 §6.5 — the input-space route inherits the composition
           - problem it was chosen to avoid                              
      2766 -                                                             
      2767 -The rewrite of §6.5 is the best piece of self-criticism in th
           -e whole plan: G2a is priced at 0.2 h,                        
      2768 -runs in week 1, is *expected to fail*, and the standing publi
           -cation rule pre-commits to never                             
      2769 -printing a 1e−3 radius as a certificate. Keep all of that.   
      2770 -                                                             
      2771 -But the inversion — "input-space vMF smoothing over normalize
           -d image directions is the primary                            
      2772 -route ... it yields a genuine, uncomposed input-space certifi
           -ed set ... and it needs no Lipschitz                         
      2773 -assumption at all" — is not right. Smoothing over **normalize
           -d image directions** certifies a                             
      2774 -geodesic ball on S^{3071}, in a space where the image's norm 
           -has been discarded. The threat model                         
      2775 -everyone else uses is an L∞ or L2 ball in *pixel* space. Mapp
           -ing a geodesic radius θ on the                               
      2776 -normalized-image sphere back to a pixel-space L2 radius requi
           -res bounding ‖x‖ and handling the                            
      2777 -normalization map's own behaviour — i.e. a composition with t
           -he same structure as the latent-space                        
      2778 -composition the section rejects, just with a friendlier const
           -ant (an angular radius θ maps to roughly                     
      2779 -2 sin(θ/2)·‖x‖, which for CIFAR may actually clear 0.25 — but
           - it has to be computed, not assumed).                        
      2780 -Two required changes: (i) state the normalized-sphere → pixel
           --space composition explicitly and                            
      2781 -compute the constant; (ii) apply the standing 0.25-L2 rule to
           - this route too, not only to the latent                      
      2782 -route.                                                       
      2783 -                                                             
      2784 -Two smaller notes. The claim that N = 1e4 "caps the maximum c
           -ertifiable radius at roughly 60% of the                      
      2785 -N = 1e5 ceiling" is conservative: the Clopper–Pearson lower b
           -ound at N = 1e4 gives p_A ≈ 0.99931                          
      2786 -→ Φ⁻¹ = 3.20, versus 0.999931 → Φ⁻¹ = 3.81 at N = 1e5, i.e. *
           -*84%**, not 60%. That errs in the safe                       
      2787 -direction, so it is fine, but get the number right in the pap
           -er. And G2c's paired McNemar design                          
      2788 -(MDE ~6 points certified accuracy, ~0.02 ACR, pre-registered,
           - with the fallback paragraph written                         
      2789 -*before* the run) is the single best-specified decision rule 
           -in the entire six-section plan. Copy                         
      2790 -that pattern into the other sections.                        
      2791 -                                                             
      2792 -### 1.5 P6's seed and power holes                            
      2793 -                                                             
      2794 -The brief asks whether P6 states a power analysis. It states 
           -two — G0(c)'s MDE (0.9 clean /                               
      2795 -1.6–2.5 robust at 3 seeds, from a correctly-derived Cohen's d
           - ≈ 3.1) and G2c's paired MDE. Both are                       
      2796 -honest. But **the primary Tier-2 robustness claim has no powe
           -r analysis at all**, and two Tier-1                          
      2797 -items are single-seed while the gates they feed demand seed C
           -Is:                                                          
      2798 -                                                             
      2799 -- **T1.7 has one seed per cell.** "Three displacement levels 
           -× two noise families × train-time and                        
      2800 -  test-time is 6 training runs" — that is 3 × 2 = 6 cells, on
           -e run each. Yet **G1(f)** requires "a                        
      2801 -  vMF-vs-Gaussian robust-accuracy gap ≥ 2 points at some disp
           -lacement level, **outside the seed                           
      2802 -  CI**". There is no seed CI. This also contradicts §6.7's "3
           - seeds on every CIFAR-10/CIFAR-100 arm".                     
      2803 -- **T1.2 (Tiny-ImageNet) and T1.3 (ViT-S) are 1 seed**, and §
           -6.8 claims as an offset that it                              
      2804 -  "dropp[ed] the two single-seed Tiny-ImageNet arms, which ca
           -rry no seed CI and therefore could                           
      2805 -  never have supported a robustness claim" — while the Tier-1
           - table still lists them and §6.7 still                       
      2806 -  says "1–2 on Tiny-ImageNet and ViT-S". One of those three s
           -tatements is stale.                                          
      2807 -- **T1.4 (the `p`-sweep), the section's best experiment, is 2
           - seeds** across 5 dimensions, and G1(g)                      
      2808 -  plus the Tier-2 unlock both depend on it. Two seeds on the 
           -*most* defensible item and three on the                      
      2809 -  least defensible one is backwards. Move a seed from T1.1 to
           - T1.4.                                                       
      2810 -- **T2.1/T2.2/T2.4 have no MDE.** The headline — normalized v
           -s standard AutoAttack robust accuracy at                     
      2811 -  matched clean accuracy — is 3 seeds per arm, and AA robust-
           -accuracy seed SD at ResNet-18/CIFAR-10                       
      2812 -  Fast-AT scale is typically 0.8–1.5 points, giving MDE ≈ 2.5
           -–4.7 points. State it. Against the                           
      2813 -  literature's typical claimed normalization effect (1–3 poin
           -ts), the tier is probably underpowered;                      
      2814 -  if it is, say so in the caption rather than discovering it 
           -at review.                                                   
      2815 -                                                             
      2816 -### 1.6 P6 compute inconsistencies                           
      2817 -                                                             
      2818 -- **CIFAR-100 is priced at 2.14× CIFAR-10 for the same archit
           -ecture and the same 50,000 images.**                         
      2819 -  T0.3 (CIFAR-10, 200 ep, 2 arms × 3 seeds) = 4.2 h; T1.1 (CI
           -FAR-100, 200 ep, 2 arms × 3 seeds) =                         
      2820 -  9.0 h. T2.1 (Fast AT, CIFAR-10) = 4.2 h; T2.2 (Fast AT, CIF
           -AR-100) = 9.0 h. Same images, same                           
      2821 -  ResNet-18, only the head width differs. Either CIFAR-10 is 
           -2× underestimated or CIFAR-100 is 2×                         
      2822 -  over-budgeted; ~9 h hangs on it.                           
      2823 -- **The AT cost model is internally inconsistent by ~3×.** §6
           -.6 says PGD AT "lands at roughly 0.7                         
      2824 -  GPU-h per ResNet-18/CIFAR-10 run against **~7 h** for the P
           -GD version"; the table prices T2.3                           
      2825 -  (PGD-7 AT, 2 runs) at **5.0 h**, i.e. 2.5 h/run. And T0.3's
           - clean 200-epoch run at 0.7 h/run                            
      2826 -  implies Fast AT at 50 epochs should be ~0.35 h, not 0.7. St
           -ate epochs and the per-epoch multiplier                      
      2827 -  on every AT line.                                          
      2828 -- **§6.4's matched-displacement κ values mix two approximatio
           -ns.** κ = 25,550 and 2,555 at A = 0.99                       
      2829 -  and 0.9 come from the large-κ form (p−1)/(2(1−A)); κ = 341 
           -at A = 0.5 comes from Banerjee. Banerjee                     
      2830 -  at A = 0.9 gives 2,421 — a 5% disagreement. Since the entir
           -e experiment is "at matched A_p", solve                      
      2831 -  A_p(κ) = A numerically through the CUSF path. You have it, 
           -it costs nothing, and mismatching the                        
      2832 -  displacement by 5% is exactly what the vMF-vs-Gaussian comp
           -arison is sensitive to.                                      
      2833 -                                                             
      2834 ----                                                          
      2835 -                                                             
      2836 -## 2. What is now FIXED — the parts that are safe            
      2837 -                                                             
      2838 -Say this out loud, because three of these were the previous r
           -ound's most damaging findings.                               
      2839 -                                                             
      2840 -- **P3's estimand problem is genuinely fixed, and fixed the r
           -ight way.** §3.6 does not buy seeds; it                      
      2841 -  fixes the analysis and then **demotes the interaction to a 
           -secondary, explicitly-underpowered,                          
      2842 -  non-gating quantity** with its MDE printed beside it, and p
           -re-commits the write-up for a null. The                      
      2843 -  paragraph beginning "So the interaction is demoted" is the 
           -best piece of statistical writing in the                     
      2844 -  plan. The 12-seed over-correction is gone (5 at 4B, 3 at 9B
           -), and §3.8's cut order — ranks and                          
      2845 -  steps before seeds, seeds last — is right. This is now a de
           -fensible design; its remaining problem                       
      2846 -  is ρ (§3.2), not the estimand.                             
      2847 -- **The compute plan is no longer incoherent by 3–16×.** The 
           -per-section budgets now sum to within                        
      2848 -  ~10% of the escalation figure instead of 3×. The overview's
           - "Read this first" item 4 (~20,900 GPU-h                     
      2849 -  vs ~6,250) is stale and should be deleted.                 
      2850 -- **P2's effective-LR control is now best-in-class.** 47.5 h 
           -of per-arm sweeps out of 571 (8.3%), the                     
      2851 -  selected-LR-vs-rank curve as a *diagnostic figure* rather t
           -han an appendix table, rsLoRA as a                           
      2852 -  scaling control, a matched-effective-LR LoRA arm, and T2.3(
           -a) hard-renormalization as an explicit                       
      2853 -  mechanism-killer. Nobody in the PEFT literature does this. 
           -Lead with it.                                                
      2854 -- **P1's effective-LR control is nearly as good** and covers 
           -more axes: per-arm (η, λ) sweeps in                          
      2855 -  *both phases* at *every* tier, phase-2 selection on new-cla
           -ss accuracy only and never peeking at                        
      2856 -  old-class accuracy (an excellent and rarely-observed detail
           -), Kosson angular-update matching, a                         
      2857 -  norm-matched control, normalization-without-tangent-project
           -ion, a Riemannian control, and                               
      2858 -  wall-clock reported alongside epochs. §1.1(a)–(f) is the re
           -ference implementation for the program.                      
      2859 -- **The κ constraints are respected almost everywhere.** The 
           -n = 1 / R̄ = 1 division-by-zero trap is                       
      2860 -  stated and honoured in P1 §1.2(6), P2 §2.7(f), P3 §3.7, P4 
           -§4.1, P5 §5.7 and P6 §6.3 — six for six.                     
      2861 -  The CUSF/torch log-Bessel requirement at p ≥ 1024 is stated
           - in all six, with correct Bessel orders.                     
      2862 -  The matched null is mandatory in all six. P2 §2.7(f) additi
           -onally gets the *right* null for a                           
      2863 -  trajectory statistic — an isotropic random walk with step l
           -engths resampled from the observed                           
      2864 -  distribution, not iid uniform — which is a subtlety nobody 
           -had to catch.                                                
      2865 -- **The stability–plasticity frontier as P1's primary object*
           -* is correct, costs nothing, and Risk 1b                     
      2866 -  ("the arms share a frontier") is exactly the right null to 
           -name in advance.                                             
      2867 -- **P4's §4.4(c) rewrite is correct.** The known-mean estimat
           -or has no leading-order upward bias and                      
      2868 -  κ̂_null ≈ √(p/n); the arithmetic checks out (at p = 512, κ ≈
           - 60, n = 10⁷: A_p ≈ 0.116,                                   
      2869 -  A′_p ≈ 1.9e−3, sd(κ̂) = (nA′)^{−1/2} ≈ 0.007, relative preci
           -sion ~1e−4 as claimed). Recognising                          
      2870 -  that this makes an NHST meaningless and pre-registering a *
           -*tolerance band** instead is exactly                         
      2871 -  right and will survive review.                             
      2872 -- **P6's gate ordering, standing publication rule, and Tier-0
           - black-box screen** (§1.3) are real                          
      2873 -  improvements over the version they replaced, and the G2c pa
           -ired-McNemar design should be copied.

      2877 -## 3. Statistical power and seed-count realism — both directi
           -ons                                                          
      2878 -                                                             
      2879 -### 3.1 Seed counts are, with one exception, now in line with
           - practice                                                    
      2880 -

      2882 -|---|---|---|                                                
      2883 -| P1 MLP tier | 5 partitions × 5 seeds | Acceptable — partiti
           -ons are replication, not seeds, and runs cost 0.035 h. 5 × 3 
           -would lose almost nothing |                                  
      2884 -| P1 ResNet/CIFAR | 3 orders × 3 seeds | Right |             
      2885 -| P1 ImageNet | 2 orders × 3 seeds | Right, and honestly labe
           -lled "confirmation, not establishment" |                     
      2886 -| P1 PTM | 5 / 3 | Right |                                   
      2887 -| P2 vision | 3 | Right, and the VTAB-19 replication is the p
           -attern the rest of the program should copy |                 
      2888 -| P2 language 4B / 9B / GSM8K | 5 / 2 / 3 | Right, and the 2-
           -vs-5 argument at 9B is correct |                             
      2889 -| P3 vision / 4B / 9B | 5 / 5 / 3 | Right |

           -t is a trend |

      2892 -| P6 CIFAR arms | 3 | Right — except T1.7 (1), T1.2/T1.3 (1),
           - T1.4 (2). §1.5 |                                            
      2893 -                                                             
      2894 -Nothing anywhere asks for more than 5 except P5, and the 12-s
           -eed design is gone. That part of the                         
      2895 -over-correction is genuinely closed.                         
      2896 -                                                             
      2897 -### 3.2 P3 — the CRN assumption is the whole ball game and it
           - is not verifiable as budgeted                               
      2898 -                                                             
      2899 -§3.6's four pooling mechanisms are all legitimate, and the se
           -ction is unusually honest about which of                     
      2900 -them *don't* buy power ("VTAB-19's 19 tasks are 19 independen
           -t training runs; MBPP-Plus's 378                             
      2901 -problems are 378 evaluations of a single training run" — corr
           -ect, and exactly the mistake a lazier                        
      2902 -plan would have made). But mechanism 2, common random numbers
           -, is doing most of the work: the primary                     
      2903 -MDE moves from 2.9 (naive) to 1.4 almost entirely on ρ = 0.7.
      2904 -                                                             
      2905 -Three problems.                                              
      2906 -                                                             
      2907 -1. **ρ = 0.7 is a stochastic-simulation number, not a deep-le
           -arning number.** CRN works when the                          
      2908 -   random stream drives the same events in both systems. LoRA
           - and DeLoRA-PC have different parameter                      
      2909 -   shapes and different early dynamics *by construction* (P2 
           -§2.1 point 2: DeLoRA-PC has a non-zero                       
      2910 -   gradient at init where LoRA's A is frozen for the first st
           -ep). Sharing data order helps; sharing                       
      2911 -   "rollout RNG streams" is nearly meaningless once the polic
           -ies' logits diverge, because inverse-CDF                     
      2912 -   sampling from different distributions yields different tok
           -ens within a handful of steps. A                             
      2913 -   realistic planning value is ρ ≈ 0.2–0.4.                  
      2914 -2. **ρ̂ cannot be measured from the budgeted design.** §3.6 sa
           -ys "ρ̂ is measured from the calibration                       
      2915 -   runs before unblinding" and §3.9 says "if ρ̂ < 0.55 the pub
           -lished MDEs are revised upward before                        
      2916 -   unblinding". T1.1 is 4 arms × 4 LRs × **1 seed**; T1.2 lik
           -ewise. A cross-arm correlation across                        
      2917 -   seeds requires ≥3 seeds per arm. As budgeted, ρ̂ first beco
           -mes estimable partway through T1.3 —                         
      2918 -   after ~40% of a 125-hour block.                           
      2919 -3. **At ρ = 0.4 the primary is underpowered against the modal
           - effect.** MDE 1.9 (the section's own                        
      2920 -   number) against Project 2's Case-B modal SFT gap of +1…+2.
           -5, with the RLVR gap presumed comparable                     
      2921 -   or larger. That is the same shape of problem the first rev
           -iew found, relocated.                                        
      2922 -                                                             
      2923 -**Fix, and it is cheap.** Insert a ρ-estimation block: 2 core
           - arms × 3 seeds at 40% run length = 6                        
      2924 -runs × 2.5 h = **15 GPU-h**, before T1.3. Publish ρ̂ and the r
           -evised MDE. If ρ̂ < 0.5, the                                  
      2925 -pre-registered response should be written *now* — the honest 
           -options are (a) accept a 1.9-point MDE                       
      2926 -and rewrite Gate 1(a)'s threshold accordingly, or (b) add 2 s
           -eeds at 4B (+62.5 h with twins and                           
      2927 -evals), which keeps you at 7 seeds and is already at the edge
           - of practice. Do not discover this at                        
      2928 -step 300.                                                    
      2929 -                                                             
      2930 -**A second, structural point.** The well-powered primary pool
           -s r ∈ {1, 2}. At r = 1, per-component                        
      2931 -scaling **is** a global scalar — P2's own `test_reduces_to_de
           -lora_at_r1` proves it. So half of the                        
      2932 -primary contrast's replication is a measurement of **DeLoRA v
           -s LoRA**, i.e. of Bini et al.'s                              
      2933 -published method, not of the novel delta. The novel delta (H3
           -, r = 2 only, MDE 1.6) is the thing                          
      2934 -Gate 1(b) blocks on, and it is the *less* powered of the two.
           - State that asymmetry explicitly: "we                        
      2935 -power a claim about prior art to 1.4 points and gate on a cla
           -im about our own delta at 1.6."                              
      2936 -                                                             
      2937 -### 3.3 P2 — the MDE arithmetic checks out, but the estimand 
           -should be named                                              
      2938 -                                                             
      2939 -`sqrt(τ²/K + σ²/(KS)) × 2.80`: vision, τ = 0.8, σ = 0.7, K = 
           -19, S = 3 → 0.576 ✓ (stated 0.58).                           
      2940 -Language 4B, τ = 1.0, σ = 1.2, K = 8, S = 5 → 1.123 ✓ (stated
           - 1.12). 9B, S = 2 → 1.30 ✓. The 9B                           
      2941 -S = 5 row is stated as 1.06 but the same model gives 1.12 — a
           - 5% table inconsistency that, notably,                       
      2942 -*strengthens* the section's argument: 2 → 5 seeds buys 0.18 p
           -oints, not 0.24.                                             
      2943 -                                                             
      2944 -The substantive point: including `τ²/K` makes the estimand **
           -"the effect on a randomly drawn task                         
      2945 -from this population"**. For the claim actually being made — 
           -"per-component beats global on the                           
      2946 -commonsense suite" — the fixed-task estimand drops the τ² ter
           -m and gives MDE = 2.80·σ/√(KS) = 0.53 at                     
      2947 -4B and **0.84 at 9B with 2 seeds**, materially better than 1.
           -30. Which estimand you want is a real                        
      2948 -choice (random-task is more conservative and more generalizab
           -le), but the section must name it,                           
      2949 -because a reviewer who does the arithmetic the other way will
           - think the test is mis-specified. This                       
      2950 -also sharpens §2.8's central argument: under the fixed-task e
           -stimand, seeds *do* keep buying power at                     
      2951 -9B, and "τ²/K dominates and no seed count removes it" is esti
           -mand-dependent, not a fact.                                  
      2952 -                                                             
      2953 -The commitment to re-issue the MDE table from the measured `r
           -=1` null spread before Gate 1 (§2.12) is                     
      2954 -the right practice — but note that a spread estimated from **
           -3 seeds** has ~50% coefficient of                            
      2955 -variation, so compute it as the sd of the paired per-task dif
           -ference across 19 tasks × 3 seeds (57                        
      2956 -paired observations), not as the sd of 3 task-averaged number
           -s. Same for Gate 0's "2× the `r=1` null                      
      2957 -spread".                                                     
      2958 -                                                             
      2959 -### 3.4 P1 — a power *note*, but no MDE anywhere, while every
           - gate is stated in accuracy points                           
      2960 -                                                             
      2961 -§1.1 says: "at n = 6 cells (ImageNet), a paired Wilcoxon has 
           -~0.8 power only for effects ≳ 1.5                            
      2962 -σ_paired". It never states σ_paired. Every gate in the sectio
           -n is a point threshold — Gate 0 at                           
      2963 -≥3.0 points, Gate 1 at ≥2.0, Gate 2→3b at ≥2.0, Gate 2→4 at ≥
           -2.0 — and none carries a variance                            
      2964 -estimate, so **none can be checked for reachability in advanc
           -e.** Back of envelope: at 25 paired                          
      2965 -cells with a CIL paired-difference sd of ~2 points, Gate 0's 
           -MDE is ~1.1 points and the 3.0-point                         
      2966 -threshold is comfortably powered; at the 6-cell ImageNet tier
           - with σ_Δ ≈ 1.5, the MDE is ~1.7 points                      
      2967 -and the 2.0-point threshold is marginal. Both are acceptable 
           -answers — but P1 should print the table,                     
      2968 -because "we set our gate at 2.0 points" invites "what could y
           -ou have detected?", and Tier 3 is 53.5%                      
      2969 -of the section's budget.                                     
      2970 -                                                             
      2971 -**Concrete ask:** add an MDE column to §1.7.6, populated from
           - Tier 0's measured σ_Δ before Tier 2                         
      2972 -starts. Tier 0 produces 25 paired cells; you will know σ_Δ in
           - week 1.                                                     
      2973 -                                                             
      2974 -### 3.5 The tiny-n rank correlations                         
      2975 -                                                             
      2976 -| Gate | Statistic | n | Stated threshold | Actual 5% critica
           -l value | Verdict |                                          
      2977 -|---|---|---|---|---|---|                                    
      2978 -| P3 Gate 0B(c) | Spearman over `G ∈ {2,4,8,16}` | **4** | ρ

      2982 -Two of the three (P1, P3) are also *mechanism* claims — co-va

      2989 -                                                             
      2990 -### 3.6 P5 — the one section over-buying seeds               
      2991 -                                                             
      2992 -§5.7's Tier 1 is 1,054 runs: §5.2 reproduction (100 runs, 10 
           -seeds), §5.3 dimension wall (150 runs,                       
      2993 -10 seeds), §5.6 S-VGAE grid (**720 runs**, 10 seeds). §5.7 th
           -en earmarks the 25-hour slack "first for                     
      2994 -topping [Tier-2] blocks back up to 10 seeds". This is precise
           -ly the over-correction the program was                       
      2995 -told to stop making.                                         
      2996 -                                                             
      2997 -- **Keep 10 seeds in §5.2 only.** Davidson et al. report 10 r
           -uns per cell; matching their protocol is                     
      2998 -  the point of a reproduction and is a legitimate reason.    
      2999 -- **Cut §5.3 and §5.6 to 5 seeds.** The published S-VAE/N-VAE
           - gap at d = 40 is ~1.9 nats against a                        
      3000 -  reported sd of 0.30–0.34; at 5 seeds the SE of the paired d
           -ifference is ~0.2 nats, i.e. ~9σ. Ten                        
      3001 -  seeds buys nothing. Savings ≈ 7.5 + 5.5 ≈ **13 GPU-h**, plu
           -s ~2 more if §5.3's extreme grid drops                       
      3002 -  from 5 seeds to 3.                                         
      3003 -- **Spend it on Tier 2**, which is where §5.5's falsifying ab
           -lations live and which is currently at 3                     
      3004 -  seeds — the exact inversion of where the seeds should be.  
      3005 -                                                             
      3006 -P5 also states **no MDE anywhere**. G1's criterion ("≥ 2 s.d.
           - separation at d ≥ 256, across 10                            
      3007 -seeds") is at least variance-referenced, which beats a bare p
           -oint threshold, but the paper will need                      
      3008 -the gap in nats with a CI, so compute it now.

      3012 -## 4. Compute honesty                                        
      3013 -                                                             
      3014 -### 4.1 The totals do not sum to 1,800, and they mix two card
           -s                                                            
      3015 -                                                             
      3016 -| Project | Section's own total | 075 / overview | Card |    
      3017 -|---|---|---|---|                                            
      3018 -| P1 | 400.0 (= 10 + 30 + 57 + 214 + 89 ✓) | 400 ✓ | A6000 |

           -00 |

           - 6 h) | A6000 |

      3023 -| P6 | 82.4 + 20% = 98.9 ✓ | 98 ✓ | 3090 |                   
      3024 -| **Total** | **1,895 committed / 1,981 budgeted** | **~1,800
           -** | **mixed** |                                             
      3025 -                                                             
      3026 -Two problems. First, 075 says "All figures are single-A6000 h
           -ours unless noted" and then lists P4, P5                     
      3027 -and P6, all of which are costed in **3090-hours**. P1 §1.7.1'
           -s own conversion is 1 A6000-h ≈ 1.22                         
      3028 -3090-h, so the three 3090 sections are being counted at ~82% 
           -of their A6000-equivalent weight. Convert                    
      3029 -properly and the program is ~1,850 A6000-equivalent hours com
           -mitted, ~1,930 budgeted.                                     
      3030 -                                                             
      3031 -Second, the "**~1,800 GPU-hours on one card**" framing hides 
           -the calendar. At ~1,900 hours and 70%                        
      3032 -realistic utilization, one card is **~113 days of pure GPU ti
           -me**, before any of the CPU work — and                       
      3033 -the six sections' calendars (P1 18 weeks, P2 18 weeks, P3 ~28
           - days at full occupancy, P4 12 weeks,                        
      3034 -P5 7–8 weeks, P6 3 weeks) are each written as though that sec
           -tion owns the card. Serialized on one                        
      3035 -A6000 at 0.6–0.8 research FTE, this is a 9–14 month program, 
           -not the overlapping schedule the sections                    
      3036 -imply. Say so once, in the overview, and pick an order.

      3040 -(section says 9.9). The total is ~42 either way, by coinciden

      3041 -                                                             
      3042 -### 4.2 P1's FFCV numbers — checked specifically, as asked   
      3043 -                                                             
      3044 -The epoch-equivalence argument in §1.7.4 is **correct and is 
           -the best idea in the section**:                              
      3045 -10 tasks × 16 epochs × 128k images = 20.48M presentations = 1
           -6 full-ImageNet-epoch-equivalents, so a                      
      3046 -whole 10×100 CIL sequence costs what one 16-epoch joint run c
           -osts. Keep that argument in the paper.                       
      3047 -                                                             
      3048 -The throughput numbers underneath it do not hold up.         
      3049 -                                                             
      3050 -- **"FFCV reaches 75% top-1 with ResNet-50 in ~2.7 A100-h."**
           - FFCV's headline is 8×A100 for ~20 min,                      
      3051 -  which is where 2.7 comes from. But 2.7 A100-h at FFCV's act
           -ual per-GPU ResNet-50 throughput                             
      3052 -  (~2,500–3,000 img/s at reduced resolution with progressive 
           -resizing) buys **~20 epochs**, and                           
      3053 -  FFCV's own published rn50 ladder puts 16–20 epochs at ~71%,
           - not 75%. The 75% config is ~40 epochs,                      
      3054 -  i.e. **~5–6 A100-h ≈ 13–15 A6000-h**, not 6–8.             
      3055 -- **The img/s figures contradict P1's own conversion factor.*
           -* §1.7.1 fixes 1 A100-h ≈ 2.5 A6000-h,                       
      3056 -  i.e. A6000 ≈ 0.4× A100. At 2,700 img/s on an A100, ResNet-5
           -0 on an A6000 is ~1,080 img/s, not the                       
      3057 -  **1,600** stated in §1.7.4. By the section's own 3× rule, R
           -esNet-18 is then ~3,200 img/s, not                           
      3058 -  **4,800**. That is a uniform ~1.5× optimism, and it is inte
           -rnally checkable without buying anything.                    
      3059 -- **Consequence.** Per-run costs move from 1.5 → ~2.2 h (R18)
           - and 4.5 → ~6.6 h (R50). Tier 3 moves                        
      3060 -  from 214 → **~305 GPU-h** and P1 from 400 → **~490 A6000-h 
           -(~600 3090-h)**. Still inside the                            
      3061 -  1,000-h ceiling, but the "600 hours of headroom" narrative 
           -in §1.7.6 shrinks to ~500, and the 3090                      
      3062 -  path loses most of its slack — which matters, because three
           - of the four headroom priorities                             
      3063 -  (32-epoch re-run, second ResNet-50 order, 5 seeds on the 10
           -×100 primary) are the ones a reviewer is                     
      3064 -  most likely to force.                                      
      3065 -- **Gate 2→3a's threshold is in the wrong units and may be un
           -satisfiable.** "Require ≥ 3,500 img/s                        
      3066 -  and ≥ 85% GPU util ... Below 2,000 img/s you are I/O bound.
           -" If the A6000's *compute* ceiling for                       
      3067 -  ResNet-18 at 176² is ~3,200 img/s, a machine with a perfect
           - data pipeline fails the gate and the                        
      3068 -  failure is attributed to storage. **Fix, 15 minutes:** in T
           -3.0, first measure throughput on                             
      3069 -  synthetic tensors with the dataloader bypassed to establish
           - the compute ceiling C, then set the I/O                     
      3070 -  gate at 0.85·C rather than at an absolute 3,500. This is we
           -ek-2/3 work per the timeline, so it is                       
      3071 -  genuinely next-week.                                       
      3072 -                                                             
      3073 -The 67%-at-16-epochs figure for ResNet-18 matches FFCV's publ
           -ished rn18_16_epochs config and is fine.                     
      3074 -The honesty caveat about short-schedule ImageNet being a *rel
           -ative* comparison, plus T3.5's 32-epoch                      
      3075 -ranking-invariance control run early and before the ResNet-50
           - and B500 spends, is the right structure                     
      3076 -and should be kept exactly as written.                       
      3077 -                                                             
      3078 -### 4.3 P3's 9B pricing is not physical                      
      3079 -                                                             
      3080 -§3.8 prices a 4B GRPO run at 6.25 h (37.5 s/step) and a 9B ru
           -n at 7.15 h (43 s/step) — a **1.14×**                        
      3081 -ratio for a **2.25×** model. Both phases of a GRPO step scale
           - with N: decode is bandwidth-bound and                       
      3082 -reads 2.25× the weight bytes per token, and the LoRA update i
           -s compute-bound at ~6ND. Working it                          
      3083 -through with the section's own numbers: at 9B, generating 32,
           -768 tokens at ~800 tok/s (the                                
      3084 -bandwidth-scaled value; the section's 1,100–1,300 implicitly 
           -assumes only a 1.5× slowdown) is ~41 s,                      
      3085 -and the update at 6 × 9e9 × 32,768 / 54e12 is ~33 s → **~70–7
           -5 s/step**, or ~11.7–12.5 h/run.                             
      3086 -                                                             
      3087 -Tier 2 becomes **~330 GPU-h** instead of 200.4, and P3 lands 
           -near **790 GPU-h** — inside the ceiling,                     
      3088 -but consuming the entire R6 contingency before R6 has happene
           -d. Note that **P2 gets this scaling                          
      3089 -right** for the same two models on a nearly identical workloa
           -d (5.8 h at 9B vs 2.4 h at 4B = 2.4×).                       
      3090 -Two sections of the same program disagree by 2× on the cost o
           -f the same model. Reconcile in week 1 by                     
      3091 -measuring one 9B step — a ten-minute job.                    
      3092 -

      3108 -## 5. The κ measurements                                     
      3109 -                                                             
      3110 -The four hard constraints are respected in all six sections a
           -t the level of stated policy. The                            
      3111 -violations are in the arithmetic, in one operating rule, and 
           -in one cross-section disagreement.                           
      3112 -                                                             
      3113 -**Violations and errors:**                                   
      3114 -                                                             
      3115 -1. **P1 §1.2, "R̄ has an O(√(p/n)) floor"** — wrong. For n uni
           -form unit vectors on S^{p−1},                                
      3116 -   E‖x̄‖² = 1/n exactly, so the R̄ floor is **n^{−1/2}, indepen
           -dent of p**. P4 §4.2 states this                             
      3117 -   correctly ("R̄ ≈ n^{−1/2} to leading order, essentially ind
           -ependent of p") and P6 §6.2 uses it                          
      3118 -   correctly (`R̄_null ~ n^{−1/2}`; its numeric 0.014 at n = 5
           -000 is exactly 1/√5000). The                                 
      3119 -   p-dependence appears only *after* the Banerjee inversion: 
           -κ̂_null ≈ p/√n. P1 has conflated the two,                     
      3120 -   and the sentence sits in the row that justifies not comput
           -ing per-class κ̂_c on CIFAR-100 — the                         
      3121 -   conclusion is right, the stated reason is wrong, and a dir
           -ectional-statistics reviewer will notice                     
      3122 -   in the first pass of a paper whose contribution *is* direc
           -tional statistics.                                           
      3123 -2. **P1 §1.2, CIFAR-10 per-class n = 2,500** — CIFAR-10 has *
           -*5,000** training images per class, and                      
      3124 -   P1's own ResNet-18 rows use 5,000 ("8 cls × 5,000 = 40,000
           -"). Correcting it moves n/p from 4.9 to                      
      3125 -   9.8 and flips the row from "Marginal" to estimable-at-the-
           -10p-edge — which is exactly what P6's                        
      3126 -   table says for the same cell. Internal to P1 *and* cross-s
           -ection.                                                      
      3127 -3. **P1 §1.2 vs P6 §6.2, a direct contradiction about the pro
           -gram's own headline measurement claim.**                     
      3128 -   P1: "ImageNet-1k with ResNet-18 (1,300 train images/class 
           -at p = 512) is the **only** setting in                       
      3129 -   the entire program where per-class κ̂_c is even arguably es
           -timable." P6: CIFAR-10 train at p = 512,                     
      3130 -   n = 5,000/class is "yes (at the 10p edge)"; RN-18 + 128-d 
           -head at n/p = 39 is "yes, comfortably";                      
      3131 -   CIFAR-100 superclasses + 128-d head at n/p = 19.5 is "yes"
           -. P6 is right and P1's "only setting in                      
      3132 -   the entire program" is false. Since both sections plan to 
           -publish the feasibility boundary as a                        
      3133 -   contribution, they cannot disagree about which cells fall 
           -inside it.                                                   
      3134 -4. **P6 §6.2's operating rule is stated on R̄ alone and theref
           -ore admits n < p cells.** "Report                            
      3135 -   `kappa_hat` only where `Rbar > 0.19`" — at CIFAR-100 with 
           -n = 500 < p = 512, a converged                               
      3136 -   classifier's per-class R̄ is ~0.85, so the rule says "repor
           -t it" while the table two paragraphs                         
      3137 -   later says "no". The rule must be a **joint** condition on
           - R̄ *and* n/p (the table's verdicts are                       
      3138 -   already computed that way). One sentence to fix — but it i
           -s the section's stated operating rule and                    
      3139 -   it is what a reader will lift out of the paper.           
      3140 -5. **The overview's characterization of P6 is contradicted by
           - P6.** Overview: "P6 also carries the                        
      3141 -   program's most favorable measurement regime ... κ is large
           -, κ/p is comfortable, and the estimator                      
      3142 -   works as intended." P6 §6.2's own table says per-class fit
           -s are **infeasible in 6 of 10 rows**,                        
      3143 -   including every CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet and p = 2048 cell
           -. The favorable-regime claim holds only                      
      3144 -   for CIFAR-10 at p ≤ 512 and for the narrow-projection-head
           - arms — a much smaller claim, and                            
      3145 -   incidentally the one P6's `p`-sweep exists to establish ra
           -ther than assume.                                            
      3146 -                                                             
      3147 -**Compliant and well-handled — do not change these:**        
      3148 -                                                             
      3149 -- The n = 1 / R̄ = 1 prohibition, with "per-input statistics a
           -re vMF *likelihoods*, never estimated                        
      3150 -  κ", is stated correctly in all six sections. P6 §6.3 goes f
           -urther and notes the resulting score is                      
      3151 -  affine in cosine similarity for a fixed class — pre-empting
           - the reviewer, which is the right move.                      
      3152 -- The p ≥ 1024 → CUSF/torch requirement is stated in all six,
           - with correct Bessel orders (P2 §2.7(f):                     
      3153 -  384→191, 768→383, 1024→511, 2560→1279, 4096→2047, 12288→614
           -3 — all correct).                                            
      3154 -- P2 §2.7(f)'s refusal to report per-layer κ̂ over `r` directi
           -ons, and its choice of **trajectory                          
      3155 -  pooling** as the primary readout because it does not degrad
           -e with model size, is the smartest                           
      3156 -  handling of the constraint anywhere in the plan.           
      3157 -- P2's admission that going to 9B makes the *pooled* directio
           -nal statistic **worse** (n/p ≈ 0.28,                         
      3158 -  halved again by the block-type split) is the kind of honest
           -y that buys credibility elsewhere.                           
      3159 -- P4 §4.2's closed-form κ̂_null ≈ p/√n and the practitioner ru
           -le — "if your reported κ̂ is not several                      
      3160 -  times p/√n, you have measured your sample size" — is the mo
           -st portable single result in the program.                    
      3161 -- P6 §6.2's inversion of the R̄ thresholds (0.193 at κ/p = 0.2
           -, 0.050 at κ/p = 0.05) is correct.                           
      3162 -- P5 §5.7 restates all three constraints correctly and adds t
           -he right distinction between the                             
      3163 -  *encoder-predicted* κ_i (a model output, fine to report) an
           -d an *estimated* per-datapoint κ̂ (never).

      3167 -## 6. The effective-learning-rate confound                   
      3168 -                                                             
      3169 -| Project | Controlled? | Verdict |                          
      3170 -|---|---|---|                                                
      3171 -| **P1** | Yes, comprehensively | **Fixed.** §1.1(a)–(f) is t
           -he program's reference. One gap below |                      
      3172 -| **P2** | Yes, 8.3% of budget | **Fixed**, and it is the sec
           -tion's best selling point |                                  
      3173 -| **P3** | Mostly | Partial — see below |                    
      3174 -| **P6** | **No, where it matters** | **Broken.** §1.3 |     
      3175 -                                                             
      3176 -**P1's one gap.** Section 07's ELR protocol Tier 2 specifies 
           -five arms including "(v) unnormalized +                      
      3177 -a **scale-invariant optimizer (LARS/LAMB)** as the effective-
           -LR-controlled reference". P1's controls                      
      3178 -(a)–(f) include a norm-matched-WD control, a normalization-wi
           -thout-tangent-projection control and a                       
      3179 -Riemannian control, but not the LARS/LAMB reference — silentl
           -y dropped. It is the cheapest remaining                      
      3180 -ELR control (one extra arm at MLP scale is ~0.2 GPU-h) and it
           - is the one that most directly asks "can                     
      3181 -you get the benefit from a step-size rule alone, with no sphe
           -re at all?". Add it to T1.2.                                 
      3182 -                                                             
      3183 -**P3's partial control.** Four LR points per arm per objectiv
           -e at **40% run length**, with a                              
      3184 -full-length re-run at the selected LR — good. Two residual is
           -sues. (i) LR ranking at 40% of an RL run                     
      3185 -is a much weaker proxy than at 40% of an SFT run, because GRP
           -O reward curves are non-monotone and                         
      3186 -frequently re-order late; P1 explicitly *checks* its shortene
           -d-proxy assumption (T3.5), P3 does not.                      
      3187 -(ii) R3's mitigation — "re-running the two best LRs at full l
           -ength for the two core arms (inside                          
      3188 -T1.3/T1.4's seed budget as seeds 1–2)" — spends seeds to buy 
           -LR validation, on a design whose primary                     
      3189 -MDE already depends on having five of them, and it covers onl
           -y 2 of 4 arms. Budget the proxy check                        
      3190 -separately (2 arms × 2 LRs × full length ≈ 25 h) or list it a
           -s a stated limitation.                                       
      3191 -                                                             
      3192 -**P6's failure, restated because it is the biggest ELR hole i
           -n the program.** T0.4's sweep is on                          
      3193 -clean, 60-epoch training. The headline models are 200-epoch c
           -lean (T0.3) and 50-epoch Fast-AT                             
      3194 -(T2.1/T2.2). Adversarial training has a materially different 
           -optimal LR from clean training, and the                      
      3195 -normalized-vs-standard comparison the paper is *about* is mad
           -e on the AT arms, which never get their                      
      3196 -own sweep. Cost to fix: 5 LRs × 2 arms × 0.7 h ≈ **7 GPU-h**,
           - against 16.5 h of slack. The section's                      
      3197 -own sentence — "at this budget there is no excuse for ignorin
           -g it" — applies to itself.                                   
      3198 -                                                             
      3199 ----                                                          
      3200 -

⏺ Docs cleaned up. docs/research_program.md is now a 1-page index; each project
  is a self-contained brief in docs/projects/; v1 and the old roadmap are in
  docs/archive/. The retired-claims table in 00-shared-constraints.md is the
  important part — it records every claim that was verified false, with the
  correction, so no agent rediscovers them.

  Here are the six prompts, one per agent. Each is self-contained for a fresh
  terminal in this repo.

  ---
  Agent 1 — Continual learning

  ▎ You are implementing Project 1 of a research program on hyperspherical 
  ▎ representations. Read docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md and 
  ▎ docs/projects/01-continual-learning.md in full before writing any code. Your
  ▎ first deliverable is Tier 0: replicate the origin result (nGPT-style 
  ▎ normalized MLP vs tuned MLP on the 5+5 CIFAR-10 class-incremental split, no 
  ▎ rehearsal), with per-arm learning-rate and weight-decay sweeps — no arm is 
  ▎ ever compared at a shared LR. Report the stability–plasticity frontier, not 
  ▎ endpoint accuracies. Then run Tier 1's effective-learning-rate controls, 
  ▎ which decide whether the effect is real: the weight-decay dose–response and 
  ▎ the LARS reference arm, both cheap. Do not implement "rotation-clamped SGD" 
  ▎ — it is LARS in disguise and reintroduces the confound it exists to remove; 
  ▎ the brief specifies the correct mediation experiment (a differentiable 
  ▎ penalty on old-class feature displacement at matched step size). Report 
  ▎ honestly if the replication fails.

  ---
  Agent 2 — Adapter learning-rate band

  ▎ You are implementing Project 2 of a research program on hyperspherical 
  ▎ representations. Read docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md and 
  ▎ docs/projects/02-adapter-lr-band.md in full first. Also read 
  ▎ papers/nLoRA.pdf — it is the project owner's own supervised master's thesis,
  ▎ unpublished, code not retained, so you are re-establishing its numbers, not
  ▎ defending them. Build the PEFT fork with five arms (LoRA, LoRA+scalar, 
  ▎ LoRA+diag(s), DeLoRA, DeLoRA-PC) and a unit test asserting DeLoRA-PC ≡ 
  ▎ DeLoRA at r=1 — that identity is the project's free exact null. Headline 
  ▎ estimand is the learning-rate band width in decades, measured on a common 
  ▎ wide dense grid ({1e-5…1e-1}, 1/3-decade spacing) run for every arm 
  ▎ including where LoRA diverges. The primary robustness check is mandatory: 
  ▎ re-express both bands in effective-step units (‖ΔW_{t+1}−ΔW_t‖_F/‖W₀‖_F); if
  ▎ the bands coincide there, the finding is a reparameterization artifact and 
  ▎ you must say so. Do not test Appendix A's prediction — Theorem 1 is false 
  ▎ and the brief shows why.

  ---
  Agent 3 — RLVR on code

  ▎ You are implementing Project 3 of a research program on hyperspherical 
  ▎ representations. Read docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md and 
  ▎ docs/projects/03-rlvr-code.md in full first. This project is gated on 
  ▎ Project 2's vision result — confirm that gate opened before spending. Two 
  ▎ things happen before any main run: (a) measure the CRN correlation ρ̂ with 2 
  ▎ arms × 3 seeds at 40% run length (~15 GPU-h) and pre-register your response 
  ▎ to ρ̂ < 0.5; (b) measure one real Qwen3.5-9B GRPO step — the plan's 1.14× 
  ▎ multiplier over 4B is not physical, the honest band is 1.8–2.4×. Build the 
  ▎ dose axis inside RL as reward granularity (k visible tests ∈ {1,2,4,8,all}, 
  ▎ log₂(k+1) bits) with everything else held fixed — do not use the SFT→RL 
  ▎ axis, which confounds six things at once. Enforce the temporal split: train 
  ▎ on problems dated ≤2025-05-31, evaluate on LiveCodeBench ≥2025-06-01, all 
  ▎ difficulties. The existing plan has a train/test leak here; the brief 
  ▎ explains it.

  ---
  Agent 4 — Hyperspherical VAE

  ▎ You are implementing Project 4 of a research program on hyperspherical 
  ▎ representations. Read docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md and 
  ▎ docs/projects/04-hyperspherical-vae.md in full first, plus 
  ▎ papers/vmf_vae.pdf (Davidson et al. 2018). Before running anything, fix Gate
  ▎ G1 — as written it requires "κ falls while KL rises," which is satisfiable 
  ▎ only while m ≲ 0.76κ and is very likely impossible at d ≥ 256; restate it in
  ▎ terms of ρ = A_m(κ). The core new experiment is T3: a vMF latent inside an 
  ▎ nViT (normalized throughout) encoder/decoder, against four controls at 
  ▎ matched parameter count and per-arm tuned LR — including the cheap one that 
  ▎ decides the story, a conventional ViT + vMF latent with the decoder input 
  ▎ rescaled by √d. If that control closes the gap, the result is an 
  ▎ input-scaling story and you report it as one. Do not build the 
  ▎ GPT-2/Optimus-scale text VAE; the brief explains why it is 3–6× 
  ▎ under-budgeted and tests a different phenomenon.

  ---
  Agent 5 — Angular perturbation of latents

  ▎ You are implementing Project 5 of a research program on hyperspherical 
  ▎ representations. Read docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md and 
  ▎ docs/projects/05-latent-geometry-robustness.md in full first. Your first 
  ▎ task is engineering, not science: the repo lacks batched per-row (μᵢ, κᵢ) 
  ▎ vMF sampling, which gates everything here — scope it honestly, estimates 
  ▎ range from 2 days to 3 weeks. The core experiment is the angular 
  ▎ dose–response and layer-to-layer amplification matrix, then expressing 
  ▎ adversarial perturbations and CIFAR-10-C corruption as noise-equivalent 
  ▎ angular doses on the same curve. Both outcomes are publishable and you must 
  ▎ report whichever you get. Two claims in the older plan are numerically false
  ▎ and must not be reintroduced (per-class κ̂ at CIFAR-100 scale being 
  ▎ "unestimable"; the KappaFace audit target) — the constraints file gives the 
  ▎ measured numbers. If you run the detection section, it is Tramèr-compliant 
  ▎ or not at all: shared encoders, LID and feature squeezing as baselines, 
  ▎ CIFAR-10-C control, held-out attacks, and post-adaptive AUROC as the 
  ▎ headline.

  ---
  Agent 6 — κ measurement instrument (run this one first)

  ▎ You are implementing Project 6 of a research program on hyperspherical 
  ▎ representations. Read docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md and 
  ▎ docs/projects/06-kappa-measurement.md in full first. This project is a 
  ▎ dependency for Projects 1 and 5, which need it in week 1 — so your first 
  ▎ deliverable is vmf-measure with the API and table schema frozen by end of 
  ▎ week 2: null quantiles, the admissibility calculator, a log-Bessel path 
  ▎ valid at p ≥ 8192, and a refusal mode that returns "not resolvable at this 
  ▎ n" instead of a number. Validate the closed-form RMSE surface in the brief 
  ▎ against Monte Carlo before shipping it; it replaces a rectangular rule that 
  ▎ produces false positives. Do not build the 60–120-claim literature census — 
  ▎ the brief's yield model gives 0–2 consequential findings and explains the 
  ▎ three structural reasons the framing collapses. Scope the audit to the one 
  ▎ surviving attack surface: comparisons at unequal n and low κ/p, where a 
  ▎ spurious κ̂ ratio of 3.01 arises at identical true κ.

  Two things I'd flag before you launch. Agent 6 should start first — Agents 1
  and 5 will build throwaway null code otherwise. And Agent 3 is gated on Agent
  2's result, so it's worth holding until that 43-GPU-h vision grid lands rather
  than running six in parallel from a cold start.

✻ Churned for 7m 42s

  constrained, but it can be read from anywhere, so my typical setup is to      
  store weights and data on net_scratch and then copy to relevant files to      
  local scratch if they do not already exist there. Also make a memory of this.

⏺ Added to docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md (the file every agent reads
  first) and saved as a memory.

  Two judgment calls I made explicit rather than leaving to each agent:

  On the two 3090s — I wrote in a preference for using them as two independent 
  workers, one run per card, rather than DDP across both. Nearly every tier in
  this program is a many-small-runs design (VTAB-19 × 6 ranks × 5 methods, 5
  partitions × 3 seeds, LR sweeps at 13 points), where two independent streams
  give close to 2× while DDP over PCIe gives considerably less. DDP only when a
  single run genuinely won't fit in 24 GB. If your cluster's 3090s are NVLinked
  and I've undersold DDP, say so and I'll soften it.

  On storage — beyond recording the pattern, I added two failure modes that
  matter for unattended agent runs: guard the copy so repeated jobs on a node
  don't re-transfer, and use copy-to-temp-then-rename so a killed job can't
  leave a half-written file that a later run treats as already cached. That
  second one bites exactly when you're running many short jobs, which is this
  whole program. I also tied it to the concrete case: FFCV preprocessing is
  ~150–300 GB and must land on local scratch, or the ResNet-18 ≈ 2–3 A6000-h/run
  figure the ImageNet tier is costed against is unreachable.

  The prompts I gave you all begin with "read 00-shared-constraints.md," so they
  pick this up without edits.

✻ Brewed for 1m 2s

❯ For the 3090s, they can use a A6000 if they need more than 24GB VRAM or even  
  the A100. But use the smallest GPU where everything fits. State the agent     
  prompts in the order I should run them (group ones that can be started at the 
  same time)
