⏺ GPU guidance updated — smallest card that fits, with the escalation ladder and
  the cheap-fixes-first list (a run that fits on a 3090 after twenty minutes of
  engineering beats the same run on an A100, because then you can have two).

  Here are the six prompts in run order. The grouping is driven by two things:
  what's a dependency for what, and the fact that you have two cards — so the
  first wave is arranged so only one agent needs sustained GPU time immediately.

  ---
  Wave 1 — start these four together

  Only Agent 2 needs the cards right away; the other three open with engineering
  or analysis work, so they won't contend.

  Agent 6 — κ measurement instrument (dependency for Agents 1 and 5 — start it 
  first)

  ▎ You are implementing Project 6 of a research program on hyperspherical 
  ▎ representations. Read docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md and 
  ▎ docs/projects/06-kappa-measurement.md in full first. This project is a 
  ▎ dependency for Projects 1 and 5, which need it in week 1 — so your first 
  ▎ deliverable is vmf-measure with the API and table schema frozen by end of 
  ▎ week 2: null quantiles, the admissibility calculator, a log-Bessel path 
  ▎ valid at p ≥ 8192, and a refusal mode that returns "not resolvable at this 
  ▎ n" instead of a number. Most of your work is engineering plus CPU Monte 
  ▎ Carlo, not GPU. Validate the closed-form RMSE surface in the brief against 
  ▎ Monte Carlo before shipping it; it replaces a rectangular rule that produces
  ▎ false positives. Do not build the 60–120-claim literature census — the 
  ▎ brief's yield model gives 0–2 consequential findings and explains the three 
  ▎ structural reasons the framing collapses. Scope the audit to the one 
  ▎ surviving attack surface: comparisons at unequal n and low κ/p, where a 
  ▎ spurious κ̂ ratio of 3.01 arises at identical true κ.

  Agent 2 — Adapter learning-rate band (takes the GPUs first; gates Agent 3)

  ▎ You are implementing Project 2 of a research program on hyperspherical 
  ▎ representations. Read docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md and 
  ▎ docs/projects/02-adapter-lr-band.md in full first. Also read 
  ▎ papers/nLoRA.pdf — it is the project owner's own supervised master's thesis,
  ▎ unpublished, code not retained, so you are re-establishing its numbers, not
  ▎ defending them. Build the PEFT fork with five arms (LoRA, LoRA+scalar, 
  ▎ LoRA+diag(s), DeLoRA, DeLoRA-PC) and a unit test asserting DeLoRA-PC ≡ 
  ▎ DeLoRA at r=1 — that identity is the project's free exact null. Headline 
  ▎ estimand is the learning-rate band width in decades, measured on a common 
  ▎ wide dense grid ({1e-5…1e-1}, 1/3-decade spacing) run for every arm 
  ▎ including where LoRA diverges. ViT-S/VTAB fits comfortably on one 3090, so 
  ▎ run two arms in parallel across the two cards. The primary robustness check 
  ▎ is mandatory: re-express both bands in effective-step units 
  ▎ (‖ΔW_{t+1}−ΔW_t‖_F/‖W₀‖_F); if the bands coincide there, the finding is a 
  ▎ reparameterization artifact and you must say so. Do not test Appendix A's 
  ▎ prediction — Theorem 1 is false and the brief shows why. Report the 
  ▎ vision-grid result explicitly as a gate decision; Project 3 depends on it.

  Agent 5 — Angular perturbation of latents (opens with sampler engineering, no 
  GPU)

  ▎ You are implementing Project 5 of a research program on hyperspherical 
  ▎ representations. Read docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md and 
  ▎ docs/projects/05-latent-geometry-robustness.md in full first. Your first 
  ▎ task is engineering, not science: the repo lacks batched per-row (μᵢ, κᵢ) 
  ▎ vMF sampling, which gates everything here — scope it honestly, estimates 
  ▎ range from 2 days to 3 weeks, and report the real number early because other
  ▎ planning depends on it. Then the core experiment: the angular dose–response
  ▎ and layer-to-layer amplification matrix, then expressing adversarial 
  ▎ perturbations and CIFAR-10-C corruption as noise-equivalent angular doses on
  ▎ the same curve. Both outcomes are publishable and you must report whichever
  ▎ you get. Use Project 6's vmf-measure for null calibration rather than 
  ▎ writing your own — coordinate on its frozen schema. Two claims in the older 
  ▎ plan are numerically false and must not be reintroduced (per-class κ̂ at 
  ▎ CIFAR-100 scale being "unestimable"; the KappaFace audit target); the 
  ▎ constraints file gives the measured numbers. If you run the detection 
  ▎ section, it is Tramèr-compliant or not at all: shared encoders, LID and 
  ▎ feature squeezing as baselines, CIFAR-10-C control, held-out attacks, and 
  ▎ post-adaptive AUROC as the headline.

  Agent 4 — Hyperspherical VAE (opens with a math fix, no GPU)

  ▎ You are implementing Project 4 of a research program on hyperspherical 
  ▎ representations. Read docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md and 
  ▎ docs/projects/04-hyperspherical-vae.md in full first, plus 
  ▎ papers/vmf_vae.pdf (Davidson et al. 2018). Before running anything, fix Gate
  ▎ G1 — as written it requires "κ falls while KL rises," which is satisfiable 
  ▎ only while m ≲ 0.76κ and is very likely impossible at d ≥ 256; restate it in
  ▎ terms of ρ = A_m(κ) and verify numerically. Everything here fits on a 
  ▎ single 3090, so use the two cards as independent workers across the arm 
  ▎ grid. The core new experiment is T3: a vMF latent inside an nViT (normalized
  ▎ throughout) encoder/decoder, against four controls at matched parameter 
  ▎ count and per-arm tuned LR — including the cheap one that decides the story,
  ▎ a conventional ViT + vMF latent with the decoder input rescaled by √d. If 
  ▎ that control closes the gap, the result is an input-scaling story and you 
  ▎ report it as one. Do not build the GPT-2/Optimus-scale text VAE; the brief 
  ▎ explains why it is 3–6× under-budgeted and tests a different phenomenon.

  ---
  Wave 2 — start when a card frees (Tier 0 needs no κ at all)

  Agent 1 — Continual learning

  ▎ You are implementing Project 1 of a research program on hyperspherical 
  ▎ representations. Read docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md and 
  ▎ docs/projects/01-continual-learning.md in full before writing any code. Your
  ▎ first deliverable is Tier 0: replicate the origin result (nGPT-style 
  ▎ normalized MLP vs tuned MLP on the 5+5 CIFAR-10 class-incremental split, no 
  ▎ rehearsal), with per-arm learning-rate and weight-decay sweeps — no arm is 
  ▎ ever compared at a shared LR. Report the stability–plasticity frontier, not 
  ▎ endpoint accuracies. Tier 0 needs no κ measurement, so it does not depend on
  ▎ Project 6 — but the drift measurement in later tiers does, so use Project 
  ▎ 6's vmf-measure when you reach it rather than writing your own null. Then 
  ▎ run Tier 1's effective-learning-rate controls, which decide whether the 
  ▎ effect is real: the weight-decay dose–response and the LARS reference arm, 
  ▎ both cheap. MLP and ResNet-18 tiers fit on one 3090; run two configurations 
  ▎ in parallel across the cards. Do not implement "rotation-clamped SGD" — it 
  ▎ is LARS in disguise and reintroduces the confound it exists to remove; the 
  ▎ brief specifies the correct mediation experiment (a differentiable penalty 
  ▎ on old-class feature displacement at matched step size). Report honestly if 
  ▎ the replication fails.

  ---
  Wave 3 — start only after Agent 2's gate opens

  Agent 3 — RLVR on code

  ▎ You are implementing Project 3 of a research program on hyperspherical 
  ▎ representations. Read docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md and 
  ▎ docs/projects/03-rlvr-code.md in full first. This project is gated on 
  ▎ Project 2's vision result — confirm that gate opened before spending 
  ▎ anything. Two things happen before any main run: (a) measure the CRN 
  ▎ correlation ρ̂ with 2 arms × 3 seeds at 40% run length (~15 GPU-h) and 
  ▎ pre-register your response to ρ̂ < 0.5; (b) measure one real Qwen3.5-9B GRPO 
  ▎ step — the plan's 1.14× multiplier over 4B is not physical, the honest band 
  ▎ is 1.8–2.4×. On hardware: 4B LoRA RLVR should be made to fit a single 24 GB 
  ▎ card (bf16, gradient checkpointing, adapter-disable for the reference 
  ▎ policy) so you can run two seeds at once; escalate to A6000 or A100 only if 
  ▎ it genuinely will not fit, and log what forced it. Build the dose axis 
  ▎ inside RL as reward granularity (k visible tests ∈ {1,2,4,8,all}, log₂(k+1) 
  ▎ bits) with everything else held fixed — do not use the SFT→RL axis, which 
  ▎ confounds six things at once. Enforce the temporal split: train on problems 
  ▎ dated ≤2025-05-31, evaluate on LiveCodeBench ≥2025-06-01, all difficulties. 
  ▎ The existing plan has a train/test leak here; the brief explains it.

  ---
  The one sequencing risk worth watching: Agent 5's first deliverable is a
  sampler whose estimate spans 2 days to 3 weeks. If it comes in at the high
  end, that project stalls behind engineering while the others produce results —
  which is why I told it to report the real number early rather than after it's
  committed.

⏺ Added to docs/projects/00-shared-constraints.md, so all six agents pick it up
  without changing the prompts I gave you.

  Three things I made explicit beyond what you said, because they're the ways
  this usually goes wrong:

  The name collision. docs/projects/ (briefs, read-only to the agent) versus
  projects/ (their code) is one word apart, and an agent that confuses them will
  overwrite its own brief. It's called out in a blockquote right where they'd
  make the mistake.

  Keeping projects/ small while it's still tracked. Since it's staying in git
  for backup until you ignore it, I told them to keep checkpoints, datasets, and
  raw logs on net_scratch and commit only code, configs, and small result
  tables — otherwise the backup window quietly puts multi-GB artifacts in
  history, which the later .gitignore won't remove.

  What "report" means here. Self-contained HTML at projects/<name>/report.html,
  rendering from disk with no network or build step, inheriting the tokens from
  docs/benchmark_report.html and docs/kappa_report.html so the six look like one
  program. Updated as results land, not written at the end. Leads with status
  and gate decisions rather than methodology. And every claim carries n, seeds,
  error bars, and its matched null — a claim without uncertainty doesn't go in.
  I also told them to log pre-registered kill conditions prominently when they 
  fire, since a triggered kill condition is the single most useful thing you can
  see early and the easiest thing for an agent to bury.

  On chat discipline: findings go in the report; chat is reserved for blocking
  questions, decisions needing you, gates opening or closing, and anything that
  invalidates another project's assumptions. That last category matters with six
  running — Agent 6's schema freeze and Agent 2's gate both change what other
  agents should do.

✻ Crunched for 40s
